# TIDE Synth — Backlog

Ordered. A weekly agent obeys the **NEXT block below first**; only when its
platform's NEXT item is ineligible does it fall back to the topmost item that
is not blocked and matches its machine's platform role. One item, then stop.

Status: `TODO` · `DOING` · `IN-REVIEW` · `DONE` · `BLOCKED` / `BLOCKED(<id>)` ·
`NEEDS-JEFF` · `WONTFIX`

- `IN-REVIEW` = work complete, PRs open (linked in the row). A run never sets
  `DONE` on its own fresh work; `DONE` is set when every linked PR has merged,
  by Jeff or by a later run that observes it.
- `BLOCKED(<id>)` = blocked until `<id>` is DONE. **Eligibility lives in this
  column alone** — section prose never overrides it.
- `NEEDS-JEFF` rows should carry a `Default in effect:` and a `Decide-by:` line
  (template in [docs/decisions.md](docs/decisions.md)), so an unanswered
  question cannot silently become the answer.
- `WONTFIX` = considered and declined, with the reasoning in the row. Do not
  re-file it.

Platform: `any` · `win` · `mac` · `linux`

New items state three things, tersely: **Scope** (files/dirs), **Accept** (a
command or observable that proves it done), **Size** (fits one session, or
names its stages). Anything longer than a few lines lives in `docs/<id>.md`
with the row pointing at it. An agent that cannot state an item's acceptance
check before starting must skip it and mark it `NEEDS-SPEC` rather than guess.

Update this file at the end of every run. An item left in `DOING` with no
matching JOURNAL entry means a previous run died — reset it to `TODO` and note
that in the journal (claims younger than 24h are presumed live; see the run
prompt).

this file is harder in one way and easier in another. *Harder:* it defines **two member functions of the concrete class** — `SynthEditApp::isMoonbaseEnabled()` and `SynthEditApp::licenseIsActive()`, both `return false` — and a member definition needs the complete type, so a forward declaration is not enough. *Easier:* the third symbol, `SynthEditApp* theApp = nullptr;`, needs **only a forward declaration** — a pointer definition does not require the complete type, and a global's mangled name carries no type in the Itanium ABI, so the symbol is unchanged. **Scope** whatever interface EditorLib actually needs from those two members — read C11's ruling first (*“narrow it to an interface. TIDE needs no licensing.”*), and note `TideAppStubs.cpp`'s own comment explains the stubs were **already dead on their first term** at their one TIDE-reachable caller (`MfcDocPresenter.cpp:1244` reads `theApp && …`, and `theApp` is always null in a TIDE process). **Accept** `SE16_SYNTHEDIT2_DIR` can be deleted from `SE16/CMakeLists.txt` and `SynthEditSem/CMakeLists.txt`, and TIDE's sources contain **zero** includes resolving only in SE16. **Why it matters:** it is the last thing between C7b and a TideSynth that configures on its own, so it gates **C7d** and therefore C7e. **Size** small, but it sits on the licensing seam like C14 did. |

## Process hardening — from the process review, 2026-08-09

The findings, red-team verdicts, and the reasoning behind the ordering are in
[docs/process-review-2026-08-09.md](docs/process-review-2026-08-09.md). The
sequence is the decision: A1 gates everything, and cadence (A7) comes last.
Rejected recommendations are listed in that doc — do not re-file them.

| ID | Status | Plat | Item |
|---|---|---|---|
| A7 | WONTFIX | — | **ANSWERED BY JEFF IN SESSION 2026-08-17: the 7x/week cadence is DELIBERATE, and the extra same-day firings are Jeff starting runs manually. "not a scheduling problem."** So this row closes: it asked to raise cadence from 1x to 2x, and the fleet has been running 7x on purpose the whole time. **Nothing to schedule, nothing to decide, and no run should re-file it.** What the measurement was still good for, and what remains true: the task description reads *"Weekly ... (Sat 02:00)"* while `cronExpression` is `0 6 * * *`, so **the description is simply stale** — cosmetic, Jeff's to correct if he cares, not a defect. The **documents** that assume 1x are the part worth knowing about: `docs/weekly-run-prompt.md` is named for a weekly cadence, and **A8 sized journal rotation against "192 KB across 37 entries in six days" on a 1x assumption** — that sizing is the one thing here that could actually bite, and it is A8's to revisit, not this row's. **Also settled by the same answer: the concurrent-session collisions are expected**, being Jeff's manual runs overlapping, not a fleet defect — though the observation stands on its own that **the claim protocol protects a pushed DOING mark and cannot protect an uncommitted working tree** (A14/A16 are that shape). **Original text follows.** **Measured 2026-08-17 (macos): this box runs 7x/week, not 1x.** The `tidesynth-weekly-macos` task reports `cronExpression: 0 6 * * *` ("At 06:03 AM, every day") while its own description still reads *"Weekly ... (Sat 02:00)"* — the cron is what runs, and the two have disagreed silently. **So this row is not "should we go 1x -> 2x"; it is "we are at 7x, what did we intend?"** A7's red-team reasoning was that cadence multiplies journal growth, PR volume and the credential-exposure window linearly — that has been happening unmeasured, and A8 sized journal rotation against a 1x assumption. It is also the same silent-staleness class the bootstrap redesign was built to kill, except it lives in the **cron**, which the bootstrap deliberately holds nothing about — no run can observe its own cadence, so no run had ever reported it. **It plausibly bears on the collisions too:** three sessions shared these working trees on 2026-08-17, and A14/A16 both traced to concurrent sessions in one checkout; the claim protocol protects a *pushed DOING mark* and cannot protect an uncommitted working tree. **Deliberately not changed by any run** — cron is standing configuration and this row is Jeff's. **Still NEEDS-JEFF, but the question has changed.** (Also observed: a firing at 20:11 local that a 06:00 daily cron does not explain; cause unknown — manual trigger, catch-up, or otherwise — recorded as an observation, not a diagnosis.) **Previous text follows.** **Re-pointed from `BLOCKED(A4)` to `NEEDS-JEFF` on 2026-08-16 (macos), and that is a correction, not bookkeeping.** A4 went DONE this run, so by the status column alone — which is where eligibility lives — this row would have become claimable, and the next run to read it would have taken an item whose own text says the work is *"per-box manual steps (Jeff or an interactive session on each box)"*: editing three machines' scheduled-task cron. A scheduled run cannot do that for the two boxes it is not running on, and doing it for its own box only would leave the fleet staggered wrong. The blocker chain (A1, A6, A4) is now **fully cleared** — nothing technical is outstanding, so this is purely Jeff's to schedule. **Default in effect:** weekly, as today. **Decide-by:** no deadline; the A6 digests are the evidence to review it against. **A6 and A1 both landed 2026-08-13 — the remaining blocker is A4 alone, unblocked but not yet built.** **Raise cadence to 2 staggered runs per box per week.** Also requires A1 and A4 (the red team's ordering: cadence multiplies journal growth, PR volume, and the secret-exposure window linearly, so the drains come first — this row's blocker chain encodes A6, and A1/A4 are NEEDS-JEFF/BLOCKED upstream of it). The stagger is no longer load-bearing — the pushed-DOING claim protocol is what prevents collisions — so weekly is a historical artifact. The task-cron edits are per-box manual steps (Jeff or an interactive session on each box). Start at 2×, review after a month against the A6 digests. |

| A30 | DONE | any | **DONE — [#198](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/198) merged; flipped 2026-08-20 (linux) per STEP 4.** **BUILT AND MEASURED 2026-08-20 (linux, scheduled run), branch `tide/linux/A30-lessons-digest`, [#198](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/198) — third item this session, continued at Jeff's direction.** **[docs/lessons.md](docs/lessons.md) is generated from BOTH journal files by [scripts/extract-lessons.py](scripts/extract-lessons.py)**, so rotation no longer takes a lesson out of circulation, and rotation step 4 regenerates it. One line added to the prompt's reading list — **that half is the one wanting Jeff's eye**, as this row said. **Accept met:** **599 lessons from 152 entries, 0 dropped**, and the digest is **56 KB against the 223 KB of Learned sections it distils — 4.1x smaller**. **The literal scope would have backfired, and the measurement is the useful part.** This row says *"rotation copies each entry's Learned bullets into"* the digest; copied verbatim that is **223 KB today, 24% of the whole corpus**, i.e. **worse than the 192 KB that triggered A8 in the first place** — the identical trap A24 measured and rejected for a 7-day floor. What makes the cheap version work is the journal's own convention: every Learned bullet opens with a bold claim, so the claim alone is a lesson rather than a title. **Two costs, stated not hidden:** the working is dropped (a line says what was learned and where to read why), and **the file grows ~3.5 KB/day, ~100 KB/month** — affordable at 56 KB now, not by October, and the pruning lever (dropping the archive from `SOURCES`) is Jeff's call, not a run's. **One bug worth not repeating:** the first cut read only markdown list items and silently kept **72 of 152** entries — it looked complete while dropping 80. Three Learned shapes exist in this journal and only one is a list. `--check` is the regression guard; it fails when the digest is stale. **Rotation ages out the lessons, not just the narrative — found 2026-08-20 (macos) while doing A24.** Every journal entry ends with a **Learned** section, and those bullets are the durable part: *“a control built from a real name has a shelf life”*, *“`-ffp-contract` is invisible in a fast-math discussion”*, *“open the artifact”*. Rotation moves them into `JOURNAL-<YYYY>-<MM>.md` with their entry, and **no run reads the archive** — the prompt points at `JOURNAL.md` only. So a lesson is load-bearing for about a day and then silently stops being read. **A24 bounded the window at one date, which is the most that is affordable** (7 days measures 112 entries / 651 KB), so the window cannot be widened to fix this — the lessons have to leave the rotating file. **Scope** a standing, append-only digest — `docs/lessons.md` — that rotation copies each entry's Learned bullets into, plus one line in the run prompt's reading list. **Accept** rotating an entry out leaves its Learned bullets readable without opening an archive, and the digest is measurably smaller than the entries it distils. **Size** small, but it changes what every run reads, so it wants Jeff's eye on the prompt half. **Not merely a nice-to-have:** twice today a run re-derived something an earlier entry had already recorded, and this session is the shortest possible distance from those entries. |

All three platform builds were green on the head before the merge-of-main; `lint` and `guard` passed on the merged commit. **Previous text follows.** **IN-REVIEW 2026-08-21 (linux, scheduled run), branch `tide/linux/A12-halted-box-digest` ([#262](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/262)). | A34 | DONE | any | **DONE — [#338](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/338) merged; flipped 2026-08-24 (windows, scheduled run) as STEP 4 bookkeeping, PR state verified rather than assumed.** **SHIPPED. IN-REVIEW 2026-08-23 (linux, scheduled run, fifth item at Jeff's direction), branch `tide/linux/A34-scan-warning`.** `folder_has_modules()` classifies each scanned folder and the warning fires only on the populated subset. **Conservative by construction** — recursive, matches `.sem`/`.gmpi` on the suffix (a `.gmpi` may be a file OR a bundle directory), and returns true on anything it cannot rule out (unreadable, or beyond a 20,000-entry walk cap). `.xml` does not count: the engine pairs each `.sem` with an `.xml` descriptor and a descriptors-only folder loads nothing. **Demonstrated three ways, one more than this row asked for:** scan folder **absent** -> probed 1, populated 0, **silent**; **exists but empty** -> probed 1, populated 0, **silent**; **one `.gmpi` planted** -> populated 1, **fires and names it**. Splitting absent from empty is the case that matters, because **absent is what CI does** and is exactly the false alarm that made this row. Suite unchanged at **8/8**. **Seven engine-free selftest cases** cover both directions, including the ones that must stay SILENT — a one-sided test would pass a classifier that never warns. **The report keeps both lists:** `foreign_module_sources` is still the raw probe list, `populated_module_sources` is the finding. **NOT VERIFIED: the CI half of the Accept** (*"the `verify` job green with no warning"*) needs this merged and a run on `main`; locally it is silent under both the absent and empty layouts and CI's is the absent one. **Previous text follows.** **The render harness's foreign-module warning fires on EVERY run, including CI, so it is noise rather than a signal.** Found 2026-08-23 (linux) while running E1c, by checking the claim rather than the warning. `tools/render_harness.py` prints *"engine scanned folders outside `--modules`; this run does not prove the named module set is what rendered"* and annotates it *"(normal on a developer box, **never on a clean CI runner**)"*. **That parenthetical is false, measured:** the `verify` run on `tide/linux/E1c-note64` ([run 32615992719](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/actions/runs/32615992719)) emits it naming `/home/runner/.local/share/SynthEdit/modules`. The engine probes that XDG path whether or not it exists, so the warning reports the PROBE, not a finding. **Why it matters rather than being cosmetic:** three journal entries now record this warning as a caveat on their results, and E1c had to spend a run eliminating it (`XDG_DATA_HOME` at an empty dir, both runs numerically identical) to establish that a real measurement was not confounded. A warning that cannot distinguish "a stray module might have rendered this" from "a directory was looked at" makes every run's provenance claim weaker than it is, and this project's own lesson is that an always-red signal trains people to skim it. **Scope** `tools/render_harness.py` — `foreign_sources()` and the warning block. **Accept** the warning fires when a scanned folder outside `--modules` actually CONTAINS loadable modules, and is silent when it is absent or empty; demonstrated both ways (a clean run silent, a run with one `.gmpi` planted in the XDG path loud), and the CI `verify` job green with no warning. **Size** small. **Note the report already carries `foreign_module_sources`**, so a consumer that wants the raw probe list keeps it either way. |

| S14 | WONTFIX | any | **NOT A DEFECT — closed 2026-08-18 by Jeff, the same day it was measured and before anything was built on it.** **A bare DSP module is not a rack citizen and was never meant to be one.** Rack modules are **Containers designed in advance and shipped as prefabs**, added to the rack at runtime: the container carries the panel, with patch-points and knobs/sliders on it wired internally to non-GUI modules like an oscillator, and the container is what the rack draws. A module that has its own GUI — a scope — can also sit on the rack directly, and **that already works today**. **So the three bare `1 KHz Tone` modules this row was measured from are not a product composition.** They are what audio testing looks like: drop plain modules in and switch to the **structure view** to check basic audio functionality. That is a developer workflow, not something an end user does on the main screen, which is exactly why nothing gives them rack geometry. **The measurement itself stands and is still worth reading** ([docs/s14-rect-measurement.md](docs/s14-rect-measurement.md), corrected): rack mode routes placement through the panel view and a plain `CUG`'s panel setter is `CDocOb`'s empty body. **One of its findings now reads as confirmation of the design rather than a complaint** — in full SynthEdit's own prefabs the modules carrying a `panelRect` are exactly the GUI-bearing ones, and the plain DSP modules carry none. **And the container half was already provided for, under a name the measurement did not look for:** `CContainer : CUG_with_patches : CUG` overrides the rect accessors itself (`CContainer.h:60-62`) and serialises its panel geometry as **`PanelWndPosition`** (`:214`) — an element already present in the measured chunk. **Superseded by:** the prefab rack modules are **E2a**/**E2**; rack-shaped styling for GUI-bearing modules is **E5**. **Do not re-open this as a geometry bug.** **Original row follows.** **MEASURED 2026-08-18 (macos, scheduled) — the question this row asked is ANSWERED, and the fix is entirely in GATED `SynthEditLib`, filed as S15. Do not re-measure.** Full working: [docs/s14-rect-measurement.md](docs/s14-rect-measurement.md). **The answer is that both halves of the row's either/or are the same event:** rack mode addresses the *panel* rect, a plain DSP module has no panel rect, so the assignment lands on an empty base-class no-op and is discarded — and `structRect`, the only rect such a module persists, is never written and keeps its constructed zero. **The `panelRect` hypothesis is REFUTED as a symptom:** full SynthEdit's own prefabs show `FloatToVolts` and `IO Mod` with **no `panelRect` at all** and a healthy non-zero `structRect`, so an absent `panelRect` is normal for a DSP module and TIDE differs only in the zero `structRect`. **Mechanism, four independently checkable facts:** `CDocOb::setViewObRect` has an empty body and `getViewObRect` returns `{}` (`DocOb.h:89-93`); `CUG` implements both **only** for `CF_STRUCTURE_VIEW` and serialises only `structRect` (`CUG.cpp:2557,2566`, `CUG.h:32`); `panelRect` belongs to `CControl`, not `CUG` (`Control.h:23`); and rack mode places through the top-level **panel** view (`MfcDocPresenter.cpp:811`, `:1421`, and the comment at `SynthEditSem/TideApp.cpp:497-500`). Composed: every TIDE placement is `CUG::setViewObRect(CF_PANEL_VIEW, …)` -> `CDocOb`'s no-op. The `master_container` is zero for the same reason — it is a `CUG` placed the same way. **Not persistence:** S11 landed and the chunk round-trips byte-identically; state right, geometry wrong, exactly as this row predicted. **TideApp's only involvement is `Document()->rackMode = true`, which is correct and is not the bug.** A17's 2026-08-18 ruling covers a **build break** in a GATED path; this is a functional defect, so it does not reach it. **Blocked on S15.** Measured on branch `tide/mac/S14-zero-structrect`. **Original row follows.** **Modules placed from the browser get a zero `structRect`, so they persist correctly but never render — found 2026-08-18 (macos, interactive) as a by-product of S11's verification.** With S11 landed, the document round-trips byte-for-byte and the modules really are in the saved chunk — `<module type="1 KHz Tone" handle="..."><structRect l="0" r="0" t="0" b="0" /></module>`, three of them — yet the rack draws empty. **So this is NOT a persistence bug and S11 does not fix it: the state is right and the geometry is wrong.** Worth stating plainly because the symptom ('I place a module and nothing appears') is identical to the bug S11 just fixed, and a future run will otherwise re-open S11. **Unknown and worth measuring first:** whether the zero rect comes from the placement path not assigning one, or from rack mode expecting a *panel* rect rather than `structRect` — `MfcDocPresenter::getRackLayout` and `ViewBase::snapToGrid` are the rack-mode geometry, and the exported document also carries `PanelLocationCenter`. **Cheap first measurement:** place a module in full SynthEdit, export, and compare the two documents' rects — that separates 'TIDE-specific' from 'rack-mode-specific' in one step. **Scope** likely `SE16/SynthEditSem/` or the shared view code. **Accept** a module placed in the rack is visible, and still visible after reload. **Size** unknown until the measurement above. |
| S15 | WONTFIX | any | **WITHDRAWN 2026-08-18 — the premise is void, ruled by Jeff the same day it was filed.** Both options below are wrong, and so was the request for a decision between them. **(b) give `CUG` a panel rect** would add geometry to the object that is specifically *not* supposed to appear on the rack, and duplicate what `CContainer` already carries as `PanelWndPosition` (`CContainer.h:214`). **(a) route rack placement to `structRect`** would push rack geometry into the schematic view's rect and fight that same existing mechanism. **No shared-format change is needed, so the GATED framing that made this NEEDS-JEFF also goes away** — that framing was the whole reason this row claimed a ruling. See **S14** for what is actually true. **The process lesson, recorded because it cost a row and a request for Jeff's attention:** the measurement behind this was sound and the *architecture* behind it was assumed — three bare DSP modules in a test patch were read as the product's intended composition, and a two-option design fork in shared code was built on that reading. One question first, *what is a rack module supposed to be?*, would have replaced the whole row. **Original row follows.** **Rack-mode placement discards module geometry — the GATED fix behind S14, measured 2026-08-18 (macos).** In rack mode every module placement calls `CUG::setViewObRect(CF_PANEL_VIEW, …)`, which falls through to `CDocOb::setViewObRect` — **an empty body** (`SynthEditLib/DocOb.h:93`). The rect is discarded, nothing is marked modified, and `structRect` stays `0,0,0,0`, so the rack draws nothing. Evidence and the reproduction: [docs/s14-rect-measurement.md](docs/s14-rect-measurement.md). **Why this needs Jeff and not a run:** every site is in **`SynthEditLib`**, which is GATED, and S14 is not a carve-out stage — A17's ruling permits repairing a GATED **build break**, which this is not. It is also a genuine design fork in shared code that full SynthEdit also runs, so guessing is the wrong move. **Option (a) — route rack placement to the structure rect.** In `MfcDocPresenter::SetModuleRect`/`GetModuleRect` (`MfcDocPresenter.cpp:811,~825`) use `CF_STRUCTURE_VIEW` when `getRackLayout().enabled`. Smallest change, persists for free because `CUG` already serialises `structRect`, and the rack's coordinates then share a space with the structure view — which may be exactly right (the rack *is* a layout of modules) or may collide with a user's structure-view arrangement of the same patch. **Option (b) — give `CUG` a real panel rect.** Add the member plus `s("panelRect", …)` to `CUG` alongside `structRect` (`CUG.h:32`, `CUG.cpp:2557,2566`), so a plain module gains panel geometry the way `CControl` already has it (`Control.h:23`). Cleaner conceptually and keeps the two views independent, but it changes the on-disk schema for **every** SynthEdit module, not just TIDE's, and needs a compatibility answer for existing projects. **Recommendation if a tiebreak helps:** (a) is reversible in one line and testable this week; (b) is the better model but is a shared-format change. **Scope** `SynthEditLib/MfcDocPresenter.cpp` for (a), or `SynthEditLib/CUG.{h,cpp}` for (b). **Accept** after the change, a re-exported chunk carries non-zero, distinct rects per module (headless, checkable) **and** the modules are visible in the rack and still visible after reload (needs a GUI session). **Size** an hour for (a) including the headless re-export check; unknown for (b) until the compatibility question is answered. **Unblocks S14.** |
| E5 | DONE | any | **DONE — [#244](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/244) + [SynthEditLib#30](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/SynthEditLib/pull/30) merged; flipped 2026-08-21 (macos) per STEP 4, PR states verified rather than assumed.** **THE GRID IS RULED AND BUILT 2026-08-21 (macos, Jeff directing), branch `tide/mac/E5-rack-grid`, [#244](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/244) + [SynthEditLib#30](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/SynthEditLib/pull/30) (GATED half).** Jeff's ruling took a **fourth direction** none of this row's three options named: `hpWidth` **stays 15** as the visual HP, a new **`snapWidth = 3`** becomes the placement pitch, the row becomes **384**, and TIDE's standard module width is **48**. **The 3 is `gcd(12, 15)`** — SynthEdit prefers 12s, every VCV module is a multiple of 15, and 3 is the coarsest snap on which both land exactly. Measured before recommending: snap-12 is exact for only **5 of 13** common VCV widths (3–9 DIP gaps, up to 3 mm); snap-3 is exact for **13 of 13**. **`hpWidth` was doing two jobs** — `renderRack` derives rail hole pitch AND radius from it — so setting it to 3 would have given rails a hole every 3 DIPs at 0.39 DIP radius. The split is the fix. **And the rail allowance never existed:** Jeff — *"useable interior is the entire rack module surface (we don't draw mounting hardware)"* — confirmed by the render order, `renderRack` fills the background and modules draw over it. So `build-prefabs.py`'s `380-2*15 = 350` was subtracting nothing real, which is why TIDE's own prefab measured as overlapping the rails. **Shipped:** `snapWidth`/384 in `Presenter.h` + `snapToGrid` (GATED PR), `kRackHeightDips` 384 and `FACE_H` 384 (ALLOWED), MidiCv regenerated, [tests/e5_rack_footprint_probe.py](tests/e5_rack_footprint_probe.py) rewritten to the ruled model (**12 selftest cases, 0 failed**, including a 380 VCV panel and a 384 TIDE panel both passing and a 400 failing), and [docs/panel-design-language.md](docs/panel-design-language.md)'s units table. **MEASURED ON A RUNNING RACK, not just built:** the probe against a live `session.xml` now reports `TIDE MIDI-CV  w=96 h=384  6.400 HP  on-grid  fits row` — **both clauses this row could never satisfy, satisfied** — with no overlaps, and the rails visibly passing BEHIND the panel ([docs/images/e5-rack-ruled-grid-macos.png](docs/images/e5-rack-ruled-grid-macos.png)). The single remaining violation is **`MIDI In` 8x14**, which is TIDE's seeded root plumbing (V3's polyphony workaround, seeded from `TideApp.cpp` — S28 owns the line reference) and not a rack module — filed as **S28** rather than excluded by name, because teaching the probe a name would make it lie. **This closes that doc's own open item 2** (*"the rack unit is 3.2 HP"*) as deliberate rather than open: 48 is 4x12 and sits exactly on the 3-snap. **Previous text follows.** **ANSWERED BY JEFF 2026-08-20, and it is smaller than this row assumed:** *“current GUI modules can draw on the rack already. They might look out-of-place because they don't default to a standard rack-module size. But that's no blocker on the project.”* **So the premise was wrong in a useful direction** — nothing is missing from rendering; what is missing is a **default size**. NEEDS-JEFF → TODO, and explicitly **not a blocker**. **Re-scoped:** give a GUI-bearing module a sensible default rack footprint so it lands on the grid instead of at whatever size its own panel happened to be. The grid is already written down in `build-prefabs.py`'s header — `hpWidth 15`, `rowHeight 380`, a slot being a multiple of 15 wide and at most 350 tall — so this is a default, not a new convention. **Accept** a stock GUI module (a scope is the example Jeff gave) dropped on the rack occupies a whole number of HP and does not overlap its neighbours. **Size** small. **Previous text follows.** **Rack-shaped GUIs for modules that already have their own UI, so they fit the rack stylistically.** Stated as design intent by Jeff 2026-08-18 while closing S14. A GUI-bearing module — a scope is the example — can already be placed on the rack and works today; what it does not do is *look* like a rack module, because its panel was drawn for the ordinary SynthEdit panel view rather than for a rack slot. **Distinct from E2a/E2**, which are the prefab rack modules composed out of plain modules (container + patch-points + knobs wired to an oscillator and the like). This row is the other half: restyling the modules that bring their own UI. **NEEDS-JEFF because the visual language is his to set, not a run's** — a scheduled run must not invent TIDE's rack styling unilaterally, and PLAN constraint 8's no-user-skins ruling means whatever is chosen ships as *the* look. **Scope** the GUI-bearing modules TIDE offers on the rack, restyled to rack proportions; likely `SE16/TideModules/` and the modules' own panels. **Accept** a scope placed on the rack is visually of a piece with a prefab rack module beside it. **Size** unknown — a design task before it is a code task. |

So this probe loads the **real rack from `tests/hosts/v1-rack.rpp`** (Oscillator → Envelope → Output, the fixture documented at 440.0 Hz / −6.3 dBFS) and **measures the output pitch at each rate**: | rate | measured | peak | |---|---|---| | 48000 | **440.0093 Hz** | −6.29 dBFS | | 44100 | **440.0093 Hz** | −6.33 dBFS | | 48000 again | **440.0093 Hz** | −6.29 dBFS | — a shift of **−0.000 cents**, against a stale-rate prediction of **404.2586 Hz** (1.47 semitones flat). **THE READING IS SELF-VALIDATING IN THE SAFE DIRECTION, which is why 440 at both rates is a strong result rather than a suspicious one:** a plugin that ignored `setFormat`, or one pinned to a fixed rate, or a rack that kept a stale rate, ALL emit samples on the 48 kHz grid for the 44.1 kHz leg — and all three then measure **404.26 Hz**, not 440. Reading 440 Hz at 44100 is only possible if the bus rate really changed AND the rack rebuilt for it. **Three controls, because a null result is worth exactly what its controls are worth:** (1) `--selftest` measures synthetic tones including the predicted stale-rate 404.25 Hz — the two hypotheses come out **35.75 Hz apart**, and digital silence correctly yields no frequency at all; (2) each leg's own audio re-read at the other rate gives **478.9217** and **404.2586 Hz**, matching the predicted ratios to 4 dp, so the analyser demonstrably tracks its rate argument rather than being pinned at 440; (3) the legs rendered **96000 vs 88200 samples**, so they are not the same buffer. **THE MECHANISM IS CONFIRMED TO BE THE SAME ONE THIS ROW ESTABLISHED FOR VST3 AND CLAP:** `AU3_Wrapper.mm:577` reads the rate off the output bus and `:600` calls `plugin.start_processor(...)`, so `processor_holder.cpp` releases the old processor, creates a fresh one, `open()`s it and re-seeds the blob from retained bytes. Instance replacement, on all three wrappers. **What this does NOT establish, stated rather than left to be discovered:** the probe is ours, not a DAW — no Logic, no GarageBand rate change; the AU loads **out-of-process** (`AUAudioUnit_XH`, and the appex binary is measurably absent from the probe's address space), so TIDE's own `rack built for N Hz` diagnostic is **not observable from here** and the audio is the whole of the evidence; and **iOS AUv3 was not exercised at all**. **Also measured in passing, useful to the next run:** `pluginkit -a <build>/TIDE-Rack-AUv3.app/Contents/PlugIns/TIDE-Rack.appex` registers the component straight out of a build tree — no copy into `/Applications`, no first launch — which makes an AU measurable in seconds. **This does not revise M1's install story**, which is about what a USER must do with a shipped pkg; it is a developer shortcut, and it must be undone with `pluginkit -r` or it leaves a registration pointing at a deleted tree. **Previous text follows.** **DOING 2026-08-24 (macos, scheduled run), branch `tide/mac/E9-au-rate-verify`** — taking the one clause this row says is left: *"AU remains genuinely unmeasured"*. That clause defers to **R3a**, which it calls `BLOCKED(M1)` with *"TIDE builds no AU"* — both halves are now stale: R3a is DONE, M1 is DONE, and `SynthEditSem/CMakeLists.txt:163` is `GMPI VST3 CLAP AU3 STANDALONE`. So the AU path is buildable and measurable on this box for the first time. **THE CLAP HALF IS NOW MEASURED, and this row's "AU and CLAP unmeasured" clause was stale within hours of being reachable. 2026-08-22 (macos), branch `tide/mac/E9-clap-host-verify`.** The row says AU and CLAP are *"unmeasured, deliberately not claimed, because TIDE builds neither (`SynthEditSem/CMakeLists.txt:59` is `GMPI VST3 STANDALONE`)"*. **R4a changed that** — the list is `GMPI VST3 CLAP STANDALONE`, and it is no longer at that line. **Measured with a real CLAP host handshake** ([tests/e9_clap_rate_probe.c](tests/e9_clap_rate_probe.c), 21 checks, all passing): `dlopen` → `clap_entry` → factory → descriptor → `create_plugin` → `init`, then **activate(48000) → deactivate → activate(44100) → deactivate → activate(48000)**, which is precisely the bracket a DAW uses for a rate change and a stricter exercise of it than REAPER offered. Every transition succeeds. **The mechanism is confirmed to be the same one this row already established for VST3:** `Processor_CLAP::activate()` calls `plugin.start_processor(...)` with the new rate, and `processor_holder.cpp:55` releases the old processor, `:69` creates a fresh one, `:82` calls `open()`, and `:215` re-seeds the blob from retained bytes. So CLAP absorbs a rate change by instance replacement exactly as VST3 does. **AU remains genuinely unmeasured** and belongs to **R3a**, which is `BLOCKED(M1)` — TIDE builds no AU. **A far more serious finding came out of the same handshake and is filed as R9:** the descriptor reports `id="SE SynthEdit"`, and the VST3 GUID is a djb2 hash of that string — so TIDE's shipped identity is SynthEdit's generic one. That is a release-track decision, not an E9 one. **What is left of THIS row:** arguably nothing. Its Accept — *"changing the host's sample rate on a loaded project re-tunes correctly"* — was measured in REAPER for VST3 (eight `rack built for N Hz` lines following the device, playback at −6.2 dBFS / −13.4 RMS, matching 48 kHz), the one real defect underneath it shipped, and CLAP is now measured too. **Left TODO rather than closed on purpose:** closing it is a judgement about whether an unmeasured AU path matters, and R3a already owns AU. **Previous text follows.** **SPEC ANSWERED 2026-08-19 (macos, interactive, Jeff directing). TIDE prepares with NOTHING before a document exists — not an early `prepareToPlay`, not a stand-up document — and `open()` installs the silence writer instead.** The re-spec asked what TIDE should do given that copying `SeGmpiProcessor.cpp:151` would null-deref. The answer is that the early-prepare idea has no content once the crash is removed: at `open()` the processor **structurally cannot have a document** (`processor_holder.cpp:82` calls `open()`, and the blob is a queued `PinSet` seeded at `:215` and drained in the first `process()`), so a document-guarded prepare would no-op 100% of the time; and latching the rate there buys nothing, because `onSetPins` already reads the live `host->getSampleRate()` at push time. **But underneath it sat a real defect, measured:** `subProcess` was installed only at the tail of `onSetPins`, and for TIDE's pin layout a no-chunk instance gets NO pin event at all — `PinStreamingStart` is emitted only for audio INPUTS (TIDE declares none), output pins are skipped, MIDI has no default, and the empty blob `continue`s (`processor_holder.cpp:226`) — so **the plugin never wrote its output buffers**. **A/B measured in REAPER** on a project whose saved chunk is empty: without the `open()` install the log shows `host MIDI arrived BEFORE the rack was prepared` and NO silence line, i.e. `subProcess` never ran; with it, `TIDE: unprepared - writing silence to the host's output buffers`. No wrapper clears output buffers (`Processor_VST3.cpp:906-941`, its silenceFlags block is `#if 0`; AU2 and CLAP likewise), so what a host hears was whatever it left there. **REAPER is NOT exposed** — its render came out identical either way, so this is a contract fix, not an audible bug on this host; VST3 does not guarantee zeroed buffers, so other hosts are unknown and untested. **Shipped** in `SynthEditSem/SynthEdit.cpp` (TIDE's own): an `open()` override that calls `setSleep(false)`/`setSubProcess`, which also makes the existing "silence, exactly as the pre-S12 stub behaved" comment true rather than aspirational. Verified no regression: `tests/hosts/v1-rack.rpp` still renders −6.3 dBFS / −17.0 rms / 2 cables. **Original item, kept for the record:** **RE-SPECCED 2026-08-19 (macos, scheduled run) — the one code change this row had left CANNOT be done as written: it would null-dereference on the audio thread. Measured, not read — [tests/e9_buildgraph_null_probe.cpp](tests/e9_buildgraph_null_probe.cpp), with two positive controls.** The 2026-08-18 research recommended overriding `open()` "like `SeGmpiProcessor.cpp:151` does", calling it a precedent to copy exactly. **It is not a precedent, because of one asymmetry nobody checked: `SeGmpiProcessor` always has a document at `open()` and TIDE never does.** An exported SynthEdit plugin bakes `dsp.se.xml` into its bundle; TIDE's whole design is that the document arrives later as the blob, and `ls ~/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/TIDE_VST3.vst3/Contents/Resources` confirms no such file. So preparing from `open()` reaches `BuildDspGraph` with an empty document, where `SeAudioMaster.cpp:409` yields `nullptr` and **`:410` dereferences it** — the existing `if (!pElem) return;` guard sits at `:413`, one line PAST the dereference it looks like it protects. It correctly handles a `<Document>` with no `<DSP>`; it cannot handle a missing document element. **NEEDS-SPEC: what should TIDE prepare with before a document exists?** A guard that no-ops until the blob arrives restores today's behaviour exactly and buys nothing; a minimal stand-up document is a design decision, and `SeAudioMaster.cpp:421-422` asserts the first `<DSP>` child is a `Module`/`Container`, so "empty" cannot mean "no modules" for free. **Also BLOCKED in practice on **E10** — the GATED null guard — which is filed but not fixed, because `SynthEditLib` is GATED and this is not a build break.** Until E10 lands, an `open()` override is a crash waiting for a host that creates a processor before the controller has state to push. **Nothing about the rate conclusion changed: a rate change is still absorbed by the wrappers replacing the plugin, and E9 is still NOT a live detune defect.** New working in [docs/e9-sample-rate.md](docs/e9-sample-rate.md), section "Why the open() precedent does not transfer". **Previous text follows.** **RESEARCHED 2026-08-18 (macos, interactive, Jeff directing) — [#149](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/149) and SynthEdit [#50](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/SynthEdit/pull/50) (the comment fix). The row stays TODO because its remaining item — the `open()` latch below — is NOT done; what these PRs land is the research and the corrections. The row's DIAGNOSIS IS WRONG, and the correction is the useful part. Full write-up with every call chain: [docs/e9-sample-rate.md](docs/e9-sample-rate.md).** Jeff asked how SynthEdit's AU and VST3 targets handle a rate change, on the premise that it requires rebuilding the DSP graph. **The premise is right and the rebuild already exists** — `SynthRuntime::prepareToPlay` rebuilds when `generator->SampleRate() != sampleRate` (`SynthEditLib/SynthRuntime.cpp:51`), and every format wrapper drives it. **What is wrong is "the rack would keep the stale rate and everything detuned".** A rate change is absorbed by *destroying the `IProcessor` and constructing a new one*: `gmpi_processor::start_processor` (`GMPI/Hosting/processor_holder.cpp:48`) releases the old plugin at `:55`, creates a fresh instance at `:69`, calls `open()` at `:82`, and **deliberately re-seeds the blob parameter from its retained bytes** (`:215`), so TIDE's chunk arrives again, `onSetPins` fires again, and the rack is built at the NEW rate. Triggers: VST3 `setActive(true)` (`Processor_VST3.cpp:369`, and the spec MANDATES the `setActive(false)` → `setupProcessing(newRate)` → `setActive(true)` bracket, `ivstaudioprocessor.h:307`); AU `Initialize()` (`AU2_Wrapper.cpp:483`, with `StreamFormatWritable` returning false while initialised at `:978` so the host MUST `Uninitialize` first); CLAP `activate()` (`Processor_CLAP.cpp:366`); standalone `onAudioFormatChanged` (`StandaloneHost.cpp:405`). **`gmpi::api::IProcessor` has three methods — `open`, `setBuffer`, `process` (`GMPI/Core/GmpiApiAudio.h:50`) — so there is nowhere to put a rate-change callback; the rate is PULLED via `getSampleRate()`.** **Measured in REAPER on `tests/hosts/v3-midi-pitch.rpp`**, driving Preferences → Audio → Device → Request sample rate 48000 → 44100 → 48000 by hand on the loaded project: eight `TIDE: rack built for N Hz` lines, the rate following the device each time, and playback after the change metering **−6.2 dBFS peak / −13.4 RMS** — the same level the fixture gives at 48 kHz. **Not one line carried the `(rate CHANGED)` suffix, and it never can:** `preparedSampleRate` is a member of the object `start_processor` destroys, so the guard cannot outlive the rebuild that handles the change. The repeated identical `44100` lines prove it — a surviving instance with an unchanged rate prints nothing. **What is LEFT of this row, and it is smaller and different:** (1) **a fresh instance with no chunk stored never prepares at all** — `processor_holder.cpp:225` `continue`s on an empty blob and TIDE's only `prepareToPlay` call site is that blob arriving; the fix is to override `open()` exactly as SynthEdit's own glue does (`se_gmpi/source/SeGmpiProcessor.cpp:151`) and let the blob be a pure document swap; (2) `DoAsyncRestart()` alone CANNOT absorb a rate change — the `resetting` branch rebuilds from the member `sampleRate` (`SynthRuntime.cpp:388`) which only `prepareToPlay` writes (`:33`), and those members are non-atomic, written outside `generatorLock`, read on the audio thread; (3) `prepareToPlay` never joins `dspBuilderThread`, so its precondition is the wrappers' own — no concurrent `process()`. **Two comments in `SynthEditSem/SynthEdit.cpp` were wrong and are fixed with this research:** the stale-rate inference above, and *"the AsyncRestart path is unreachable in the plugin runtime"* — `resetting` is reached via `ug_vst_out.h:65` → `SeAudioMaster::onFadeOutComplete()` → `OnFadeOutComplete()` (`iseshelldsp.h:124`), and `DoAsyncRestart` is reached from `dsp_patch_parameter.cpp:773` for any host control with `requiresAsyncRestart()`, a set that **includes `HC_PATCH_CABLES`** (`dsp_patch_parameter_base.h:187`), i.e. every rack re-cabling. Nothing in TIDE calls it *yet*, which is a property of TIDE's wiring, not of the runtime. **Unmeasured, deliberately not claimed:** AU and CLAP, because TIDE builds neither (`SynthEditSem/CMakeLists.txt:59` is `GMPI VST3 STANDALONE`); and whether parsing a document on the audio thread is safe. **Original item, kept for the record:** **TIDE latches its sample rate at DOCUMENT-PUSH time and has no rate-change path — found 2026-08-18 (macos, interactive) when Jeff asked whether TIDE has the correct sample rate or a hard-coded default.** **It is NOT hard-coded**, which was the first thing checked: `SynthEditSem/SynthEdit.cpp` passes `static_cast<int32_t>(host->getSampleRate())` to `rack.prepareToPlay`, and a temporary log confirmed real values arriving from REAPER — **48000 Hz** and **44100 Hz**, block 512. **The problem is WHERE that call lives:** `prepareToPlay` is reached from exactly one place, the chunk arriving in `onSetPins`, so the rack's rate is fixed when the DOCUMENT is pushed. Nothing in the class handles a rate change. **A host that changes rate without re-pushing the document would leave the rack at the stale rate and everything detuned by the ratio — silently, with no warning and nothing in the saved project to show it.** 48000 → 44100 is 1.47 semitones, so it would sound wrong rather than broken, which is the worst kind. **What is measured and what is not, kept apart deliberately:** an offline render prints both rates, but those are two separate plugin **INSTANCES** (the device/editor one and the render one), each latching once at construction — the guard's "rate CHANGED" branch did **not** fire, which is how that is known rather than assumed. So a rate change *within* an instance has never been observed to be handled, and on this evidence it would not be. **A rate-change test needs the GUI:** REAPER ignores a project's `SAMPLERATE` line when rendering, and hand-writing `RENDER_SRATE` into a `.rpp` makes REAPER stop on a dialog (confirmed by Jeff while it blocked a render), so forcing a different render rate has to go through REAPER's own dialog. **Scope** `SynthEditSem/SynthEdit.cpp` — TIDE's own, ALLOWED. A `preparedSampleRate` guard is already in place and logs `TIDE: rack built for N Hz` on a change; what is missing is acting on it. **Accept** changing the host's sample rate on a loaded project re-tunes correctly rather than staying detuned. **Size** small if a rate check per block is acceptable; larger if it wants a proper host callback. **NOT the cause of E8:** that offset is exactly 3 semitones (ratio 1.18921), a musical interval and not any standard rate ratio — 48000/44100 is 1.47 semitones — and the Oscillator's 5 V → 440.0 Hz holds at both 44100 (via the `.rpp`) and 48000 (via E1's `prefab_oscillator`). |

| E11 | WONTFIX | any | **MEASURED 2026-08-21 (macos), [#256](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/256) — THE HAZARD IS UNREACHABLE — not a bug, and the reason is worth more than the row.** This row asked for the drain ordering to be established before any patch. The ordering turns out not to matter, because **the pointer is never taken.** Static proof, and it is exhaustive rather than a sample: in `GMPI/Hosting/processor_holder.h` the processor-side parameter's `value_` is written in exactly **two** places, both at construction — `:87` (a double default) and `:91` (`std::vector<uint8_t>{}`, an EMPTY blob) — and **`setBlob` at `:135` is a STUB**: its one assignment is commented out at `:137` and it unconditionally `return true`s. `grep` for `value_` across the header and the .cpp finds only those two writes and two reads. **So for a blob parameter the vector is empty for the object's entire lifetime**, and both sites that could publish a raw pointer decline to: the seed at `processor_holder.cpp:224` hits `if (!v || v->empty()) continue;`, and `sendParameterToProcessor` at `:337` takes the inline branch because `v.size() > 8` is false when the size is 0. **`e.oversizeData_` is never assigned on this path**, so nothing can dangle. **The genuine defect is the stub, and it is E6's, not a new row** — see the note added to E6 the same day, per A31's grep-before-filing habit, which is what stopped this becoming a duplicate. **Reopen this row only if `setBlob` is ever implemented**, because the moment the vector holds bytes both pointer sites become live and this row's analysis applies exactly as written. **Previous text follows.** **UNVERIFIED, filed so it is not lost: `processor_holder.cpp:231` publishes a raw pointer into a `std::vector` that another thread may reallocate.** The blob seed does `e.oversizeData_ = v->data()` with no byte copy, and `EventQue` stores the `Event` by value (`processor_holder.h:22-29`). The comment justifying that at `:333-336` argues "queue drain and the next parameter update happen on the same thread, so the vector cannot move underneath the event" — which holds for the `ppc3` path it was written about, but **`start_processor` runs on the host's main thread** while the drain happens in the first `process()` on the audio thread. If a `setBlob` reallocates in between, the event points at freed memory and TIDE parses whatever is there — which is also another route into **E10**'s crash. **Not measured**: found by an agent reading the code during E9's research (2026-08-19) and explicitly flagged "ordering unverified"; nobody chased the drain ordering, so this may be perfectly safe. **Take** = establish the ordering first (does anything write parameter 1 between `start_processor` and the first `process()`? a DAW state restore plus an editor push is the suspicious pair), and only then decide whether the fix is a copy, a shared_ptr, or a comment saying why it cannot happen. **Scope** `GMPI/Hosting/` — Jeff's, not in the GATED list, but a hosting-layer change with every plugin downstream, so it wants a measurement before a patch. **Size** small to fix, unknown to prove. |

The diagnosis here is sound and the measurement excellent, but the fix pointed one layer too low: `SE Label` is a GMPI module, GMPI's `"string"` IS utf-8, so `Pin<std::string>` was already correct and macOS types the pin `string_utf8` (measured from the module cache). Changing it to `Pin<std::wstring>` would corrupt macOS captions the mirror way. The real defect — linux registering the pin as DT_TEXT where macOS registers DT_STRING_UTF8 — is now S46. This row's linux-side evidence is kept because S46 builds directly on it.** **ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED — it is a datatype mismatch, not a font problem. 2026-08-24 (linux, interactive, Jeff directing), branch `tide/linux/S45-caption-tofu`, product change in [SynthEditLib#39](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/SynthEditLib/pull/39).** **The chain, from a backtrace rather than from reading:** `MfcDocPresenter::RefreshView` → `ViewBase::Refresh` → `ViewBase::ConnectModules` (`ViewBase.cpp:679`, setting GUI pin defaults) → `PluginEditorBase::setPin` → `Pin<std::string>::setFromHost` → `LabelGui`. **`LabelGui` declares `<Pin name="Text" datatype="string"/>`, `conversion.cpp:996` maps `"string"` to `DT_TEXT`, and `RawConversions.cpp:238` encodes `DT_TEXT` as RAW `wchar_t` bytes** (`result.resize(sizeof(wchar_t) * s.size())` + `memcpy`). The C++ pin was `Pin<std::string>`, whose `valueFromData<std::string>` is a **verbatim byte copy** — so `"MIDI-CV"` arrived as `'M',0,0,0,'I',0,0,0,…` and every NUL drew `.notdef`. **MEASURED, not inferred:** logging the shaped codepoints gives **28 codepoints for 7 characters, exactly 3 NULs after each** — `GATE` 16 cps, `MIDI-CV` 28, `PITCH` 20, `TRIG` 16, `VEL` 12. **`wchar_t` is 4 bytes on Linux, 2 on Windows, so this is very likely NOT Linux-only** — Windows would get one NUL per character from the same code path, and nobody has looked. **The row's own eliminations all held:** four real fonts loaded, zero `ENOENT`, and the engine reported **zero notdef events** (because the primary face was used without a fallback request, which is exactly why the notdef probe was silent while boxes were on screen). **FIX:** declare the pins `Pin<std::wstring>` — matching what the host actually sends, since `valueFromData<std::wstring>` already divides by `sizeof(wchar_t)` — and convert to UTF-8 once, at the three points that hand text to the drawing API. **THE WIRE FORMAT IS DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED:** `datatype="string_utf8"` would also work and is arguably tidier, but it changes the datatype of a pin existing patches may connect to, and this codebase auto-inserts converters on mixed-datatype connections — a silent behaviour change. That is Jeff's call and is stated on the PR. **SCOPE CHECKED:** `LabelGui` is the **only** `graphicsApi="GmpiUi"` module in the tree and holds the only two pins with this mismatch. **VERIFIED:** captions render **MIDI-CV / PITCH / GATE / VEL / TRIG** in real glyphs ([docs/images/s45-captions-fixed.png](docs/images/s45-captions-fixed.png)); module browser unchanged; zero plug-in diagnostics. **NOT VERIFIED:** `SynthEditCL` was not built (the change is confined to one module and compiles as part of TIDE, but the commercial consumer was not built); Windows and macOS were not built. **CLAIMED 2026-08-24 (linux, interactive, Jeff directing), branch `tide/linux/S45-caption-tofu`.** Taking the diagnosis: the four eliminations below are done, what is missing is which font the two call sites actually resolve. **Rack panel pin captions render as missing-glyph boxes on Linux, while the module browser in the same window renders text correctly.** Split out of **S25** on 2026-08-24 (linux, interactive, Jeff directing) after that row's own Accept passed: S25 is about a difference between the freshly-inserted and session-restored paths, and this **happens identically in both**, so it is a different defect wearing the same symptom. **What is seen:** the startup-seeded root MIDI-CV facade's captions draw as boxes with a few real glyphs among them — `-`, `C`, `V`, `T`, `H`, `E`, `R`, `I`, `G`, `.` are legible, most characters are not — with the text laid out at the correct positions and widths. The rebuild is a timer, and the timer was dead. **Mechanism:** `MfcDocPresenter` is its own `se_sdk::TimerClient` — `setView()` does `StartTimer(50)`, each tick services `viewDirty` -> `RefreshView()` -> re-export to JSON -> rebuild (`MfcDocPresenter.h:351`, `.cpp:429-455`). A view-switch works because `setView()` calls `OnTimer(); // intial refresh` directly, needing no timer. Rides gmpi_ui timers because those ARE serviced here (`StandaloneApp.cpp:332`). State queried via the API rather than inferred: `MERGED`. **Previous text follows.** **FIXED 2026-08-21 (macos), branch `s29-one-run-per-commit` — AND THIS ROW'S OWN RECOMMENDED FIX WAS WRONG, which is the part worth keeping.** **Measured first:** every `build.yml` run for `tide/mac/macos-arm64` — four shas, **eight runs**, each an exact `push`/`pull_request` pair about **four seconds apart**. Cross-sha cancellation already works (older runs show `cancelled`); it is only the same-sha pair that escapes. **Why this row's option (b) — 'drop `event_name` from the concurrency group' — does NOT work:** the group also keys on `github.ref`, and that is `refs/heads/<branch>` for push but **`refs/pull/<n>/merge`** for pull_request. Same-repo PRs are already covered by their push run, whose checks attach to the same sha and so appear on the PR. `build` inherits the skip through `needs: guard`. **Chosen over a sha-keyed concurrency group deliberately:** that variant still lets both runs QUEUE before cancelling one, and on this repo the scarce resource is the macOS runner (**S30**, ~5% completion) — a run that queues and dies has already taken the slot. This one never starts it. **Verified before handing over:** YAML parses (jobs `guard`, `build` intact, triggers unchanged), and `main` has **no required status checks**, so a skipped job cannot block a merge. **The cost, stated:** same-repo PRs are no longer tested as a merge result, only as the branch tip — acceptable while this repo squash-merges quickly. **Jeff pushes it**: the fleet token is `repo`-scope only, no `workflow`. **Previous text follows.** **Every `tide/**` branch with a PR runs the whole three-platform matrix TWICE on the same commit, and macOS is the scarce runner.** Measured 2026-08-21 (macos) while waiting on [#250](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/250)'s macOS job: `build.yml` triggers on **both** `push: branches: ["main", "tide/**"]` **and** `pull_request:`, so a PR from a `tide/**` branch fires the matrix once for the push and once for the PR — same sha, same result. **Of the last 40 `build.yml` runs, 24 were `push` and 16 `pull_request`**, and `tide/mac/macos-arm64` had two queued simultaneously when this was written. **Why it matters more than it sounds:** the queue was **8 runs deep** with macOS jobs waiting, and a universal macOS build took **160 s** locally (79 s arm64) — so duplicate macOS runs are the single largest consumer of CI wall-clock on this repo. Halving them is a bigger queue win than the arm64 change. **Do NOT just delete `tide/**` from the push trigger without checking the issue-filing path**: the *"File a platform issue on failure"* step is what tells the owning box a platform broke, and a branch pushed WITHOUT a PR would stop being checked entirely if push runs go away. That is the trade this row exists to think about, not a detail. **Options:** (a) drop `tide/**` from `push` and accept that branch-without-PR pushes are unchecked; (b) keep both but add a `concurrency:` group keyed on the sha so the second run cancels the first; (c) keep push, restrict `pull_request` to PRs from forks. **(b) is probably right** — it is one block, changes no trigger semantics, and GitHub cancels the redundant run automatically. **And the reason the duplicates survive today is exact, not vague: `build.yml` ALREADY HAS a concurrency block** (`:55-57`), but its group is `${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.event_name }}-${{ github.ref }}` — **`event_name` is in the key**, so the `push` run and the `pull_request` run of the same commit land in DIFFERENT groups and cannot cancel each other. Dropping `event_name` from the group is the whole fix. **Scope** `.github/workflows/build.yml` — **a workflow file, so Jeff pushes it**; the fleet token has no `workflow` scope, verified 2026-08-21 (bot scopes: `repo`). **Accept** one commit produces one matrix run. **Size** minutes. |
| S30 | DONE | any | **DONE — [#292](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/292), [#293](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/293) and [#297](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/297) all merged; flipped 2026-08-22 (macos) as STEP 4 bookkeeping. **The row is closed on measurement, not on the fixes landing:** macOS `Build` was 71.5 and 60.1 min before ccache and 0.3 and 0.2 min after. **The self-hosted-runner option is retired** — it was sized against a 60-minute build. **Two caveats kept rather than dropped:** the exact speedup is NOT established (the one hit-rate sample, 66.5%%, could not be correlated to the same run as the duration), and both post-ccache runs were docs-and-backlog merges whose C++ was largely unchanged, so a run that genuinely recompiles will be slower. **Previous text follows.** **ccache IS NOW IN `release.yml` TOO — a gap in the first fix, found by Jeff asking why the release was still slow. 2026-08-22, branch `tide/mac/S30-ccache-in-release`.** The S30 work added ccache to `build.yml` and **not** to `release.yml`, so every release paid the full ~60 minutes on macOS — the one place the wait is actually felt, because a person is waiting on a tag. **AND THE FIX IS MEASURED NOW, which S30 could not claim before:** macOS `Build` step durations either side of the ccache merge — **71.5 min and 60.1 min before**, **0.3 min and 0.2 min after**. **Reported with a caveat rather than as a headline ratio:** the two post-ccache runs were docs-and-backlog merges, so their C++ was largely unchanged, and the one hit-rate sample read **66.5%% (1068/1605)** — which does not square with 537 misses finishing in twelve seconds, so those two figures were probably not from the same run. **What is solid: both post-ccache macOS builds finished in well under a minute against 60+ before. The exact speedup is not established.** **The cache key prefix deliberately matches `build.yml`'s**, because a tag run can read caches created on the default branch — so a release starts warm from whatever `main` last compiled instead of from nothing. Change one prefix and both must change. **A deliberate choice worth naming: this caches a SIGNED, SHIPPED artifact's build.** ccache keys on preprocessed source, compiler and flags, so a hit is a byte-identical object — the same assumption an incremental local build makes. If it is ever in doubt, bumping the prefix forces a cold build; the comment says so at the point of use. **ANSWERS THE SELF-HOSTED RUNNER QUESTION: no runner is needed.** That option was sized against a 60-minute build, and the build is no longer 60 minutes. **Previous text follows.** **BOTH FIXES AUTHORED 2026-08-22 (macos, Jeff directing), branch `tide/mac/S30-concurrency-and-ccache` — [.github/workflows/build.yml](.github/workflows/build.yml). JEFF MUST PUSH IT (workflow scope).** **(1) `cancel-in-progress: false`.** The completion rate is a policy choice independent of the duration, and that only became visible once the diagnosis stopped being "the queue". Cancelling was destroying 61% of macOS jobs AND 25% of windows+linux ones, each costing 5–10 minutes to redo. **This does not pile up runs:** GitHub keeps at most ONE pending run per group — a third arrival supersedes the second — so a group holds one running plus one queued and the newest push always gets its turn. The cost is latency on a rapidly-pushed branch, not an unbounded queue. **(2) `ccache` on macOS and Linux.** Not Windows: ccache's MSVC support is weaker and Windows builds in 10 minutes, so it is not where the problem is. `hash_dir=false` is load-bearing — dependency sources are re-fetched into a path containing the build directory, so absolute paths differ per run and would defeat every hash; hashing content instead is what makes a cross-run hit possible at all. `restore-keys` matters more than the exact key, since TIDE's own sources change every run and the normal case is a PREFIX hit that is warm for the dependencies. `CMAKE_OBJCXX_COMPILER_LAUNCHER` is set as well as C/CXX, or the `.mm` Cocoa layer — exactly the mac-specific half — would be exempt. **A DESIGN I TRIED AND ABANDONED, worth recording so nobody re-attempts it:** per-platform cancellation via job-level `concurrency` with `cancel-in-progress: ${{ matrix.name != 'macos' }}`. **It cannot work** — job-level `concurrency` may only use the `github`, `inputs` and `vars` contexts, and **`matrix` is not among them** (confirmed against GitHub's workflow-syntax reference, not from memory). It would have parsed and behaved wrongly. **VERIFIED as far as a workflow can be without running it:** YAML parses; step order asserted programmatically (`apt-get update` before `apt-get install ccache`, ccache before Configure, Configure before Build, stats after Build); `cancel-in-progress` is `false`; and `actions/cache` was checked against its registry — **latest is v6, the first draft said v4** — with its `path`/`key`/`restore-keys` inputs confirmed unchanged. **THE PAYOFF IS STILL NOT MEASURED, deliberately.** The 56% figure is a share of compile COUNT, not wall-clock. So the workflow prints `ccache -s` on every run, including failed ones — the next few runs measure the answer rather than anyone estimating it. **Revisit the concurrency choice if ccache brings macOS near the other platforms:** at 5–10 minutes a side, cancelling would be cheap again. **Previous text follows.** **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-22 (macos, Jeff asking what he needs to do) AND THIS ROW'S DIAGNOSIS IS NOW WRONG. It says starvation from a 1h+ QUEUE meeting `cancel-in-progress`. The queue is gone; the BUILD is the problem.** **Measured over the last 25 build runs, per-job `created_at` → `started_at` → `completed_at`:** macOS queue median **0.1 min** (max 41.6), linux 0.0, windows 0.0. So the hour-long wait this row was filed on no longer happens. **What does happen:** macOS *build* median **59.5 min**, against linux **5.2** and windows **10.0**. And it is not doing more work — the same run compiled **495** objects on macOS and **496** on Windows, so macOS is roughly **5× slower per unit of work**, not busier. **That is what feeds the cancellations:** a 60-minute job has a 60-minute window in which a newer push can kill it, so macOS is cancelled **17 of 28** jobs (61%) against **14 of 56** (25%) for windows+linux. **The completion rate has improved a lot since this row was written** — **32%** (9/28) now, not the 5% recorded on 2026-08-21, almost certainly the arm64-only change plus S29 removing duplicate runs. It is still 2.4× worse than the other platforms. **THE OPTIONS THIS ROW LISTS ARE NOW MIS-WEIGHTED, because they were chosen against a queue.** (a) a self-hosted runner still works and is now the *definitive* fix rather than merely a queue bypass — the same build takes minutes on Jeff's own Mac; (b) dropping macOS from the per-branch matrix still hides the problem rather than fixing it; (c) *new, and cheapest*: **cache the build**. 56% of the macOS compiles come from fetched dependencies (`syntheditlib-src` 103, `gmpi_ui-src` 51, and the rest) that change rarely, so `ccache` or an `actions/cache` over the object tree attacks most of the hour for a few lines of YAML; (d) *also new*: **stop cancelling macOS specifically** — the completion rate is a `concurrency` policy choice independent of the duration, and the two levers are separable. **Scope** `.github/workflows/build.yml` (**Jeff pushes it**) or runner infrastructure. **NOT MEASURED:** how much of the hour `ccache` would actually remove — the 56% is a share of compile *count*, not of wall-clock, and dependency objects are not necessarily the slow ones. That is one cached run away from being known. **Also unexplained:** the linux job logged zero Build lines in the run sampled, while separately showing a 5.2 min median — a log-capture artifact rather than a finding, but not chased. **Previous text follows.** **The macOS CI job almost never runs to completion — 2 of the last 40 `build.yml` runs, about 5%. It is starvation, not breakage.** Measured 2026-08-21 (macos) while waiting on [#250](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/250). **The mechanism is two things meeting:** GitHub's hosted macOS runners for this public repo queue for **over an hour** (run 32428686229's macos job was assigned at 23:27 and had still not started 60+ minutes later, while windows and linux finished in minutes), and `build.yml`'s `concurrency: cancel-in-progress: true` (`:55-57`) kills a pending run when a newer one arrives for the same ref. On a busy day — 16 PRs merged on 2026-08-20 — **every macOS job on `main` was cancelled before it ever started.** **macOS is NOT broken, and that is the useful half:** the two runs that did complete both passed (`32411882991`, `32411834158`), and notably both were runs where **linux failed and macOS succeeded**. So the platform builds; it just rarely gets to say so. **Why it matters beyond tidiness:** C7e's Accept is *"build.yml's three platforms run rather than skip, and pass, on a PR"* — a clause that a 5% completion rate can leave unsatisfiable by luck rather than by fault, and this repo has been bitten before by a green CI that was not measuring what anyone thought (**S20**). **Options:** (a) a self-hosted macOS runner — Jeff has the hardware, and it removes the queue entirely; (b) drop macOS from the per-branch matrix and run it on `main` and tags only, accepting later detection; (c) leave it and rely on local verification, which is what actually catches mac problems today. **The arm64 change helps but does not fix this** — it halves the macOS build (160 s → 79 s measured) and so shortens the window in which a cancel can land, but the queue wait dwarfs the build. **Scope** `.github/workflows/build.yml` (**Jeff pushes it**) or runner infrastructure. **Accept** a macOS job completes on a PR without needing luck. **Size** minutes for (b); an afternoon for (a). |

AND THE ROW'S PREMISE IS PLATFORM-SPECIFIC, which is why three retellings did not stop it.** **The measurement that reframes it:** BSD `pkill` (macOS) excludes the calling process **and all of its ancestors** by default — `man pkill`, the `-a` flag — so on a Mac `pkill -f <pattern>` from a shell carrying that pattern is **already safe**. GNU procps (Linux) excludes only the calling process. Verified by trapping `TERM` in the calling shell: it traps a directly-sent signal (rc=143) and receives **nothing** from `pkill -f`, so the signal is not delivered rather than ignored. **So this trap can only ever fire on the `linux` box** — every one of the three recorded hits was linux — and a mac or windows run cannot reproduce it however carefully it tries. *"It worked when I tested it"* was true and useless. **Which forced the test design, and is the transferable part:** cases 1 and 2 (kills the target; the calling shell survives) **both still PASS on macOS with the ancestor walk deliberately broken** — measured, not predicted, by setting `p=1` before the walk. Behaviour tests cannot see this bug here. So the suite asserts the ancestor list **directly** via a `--print-ancestors` mode, which fails on both injected breaks. **Negative controls run both ways:** ancestor walk stubbed → cases 3 and 4 fail; `is_ancestor` forced true → case 1 fails (it spares everything and kills nothing). **[docs/ci/headless-gui-verification.md](docs/ci/headless-gui-verification.md) updated** — it already said *"never `pkill -f`"* and was still not enough, so it now points at the script and explains the platform split. **NOT verified: the suite has never run on Linux or Windows**, which is where the bug actually lives; the shape that worked this run was `for p in $(pgrep -x <exe>); do kill "$p"; done` and, for a script, matching `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` explicitly. **Accept** a documented command that a run can copy which terminates a named child process and, run from a shell whose own command line contains that name, leaves that shell alive — demonstrated both ways. **Size** small. |

Option (b) works and costs nothing.** **Recipe:** [docs/ci/headless-gui-verification.md](docs/ci/headless-gui-verification.md) — `weston --backend=headless --socket=tide-test`, then `WAYLAND_DISPLAY=tide-test XDG_CONFIG_HOME=<scratch> ./TIDE_STANDALONE`. The headless backend needs no display, no seat and no GPU, and **nothing it does reaches `gnome-shell`**, so the mutter teardown bug cannot be triggered by anything a run does. **Measured, not asserted:** the app came up, the rack drew, the module browser listed every group, and `gmpi_screenshot` returned a correct 1100x626 PNG — because it renders from the app's OWN buffer rather than asking the compositor, so **no verification capability is lost**. **Roughly ten launches across this session** (control, resources-absent, mismatch, four shutdown trials, plus a **continuous 635 s run — 10 m 35 s — that was still answering the command channel at the ten-minute mark**, confirmed by a screenshot taken then, and which then shut down cleanly by pid) with **zero `gnome-shell` crashes** and **zero `TIDE_STANDALONE` segfaults** and Jeff's login session (`loginctl` session 10) unchanged throughout — against four crashes in the two days before. **One cosmetic wart, documented so nobody chases it:** `Gdk-CRITICAL … gdk_seat_get_keyboard: assertion 'GDK_IS_SEAT (seat)' failed`, because the headless backend advertises no keyboard. Harmless. **Nested-but-visible (`weston` with no `--backend`) is only PARTIAL isolation** and the doc says so: the nested compositor is itself a mutter client, so its own exit still runs the path that crashes. **The underlying mutter bug is untouched and still Jeff's to decide about** — option (a). It is not TIDE's to fix. **Previous text follows.** **`gnome-shell` on the linux box segfaults in mutter’s Wayland CLIENT-TEARDOWN path, taking Jeff’s whole desktop to the login screen. Four occurrences in two days, and it is the single biggest tax on runtime work here.** Filed 2026-08-21 (linux, scheduled run) after the fourth. **Occurrences, from `journalctl` (`GNOME Shell crashed with signal 11` / `org.gnome.Shell@wayland.service: Main process exited, code=dumped, status=11/SEGV`):** `Aug 20 17:35:18`, `Aug 21 16:44:21`, `Aug 21 16:48:06`, `Aug 21 18:28:59`. **The backtrace, from the apport core** (`/var/crash/_usr_bin_gnome-shell.1000.crash`, unpacked with `apport-unpack`, read with `gdb -batch -ex "thread apply 1 bt"`): `wl_display_flush_clients` -> `wl_client_destroy` -> libmutter-14 -> `g_signal_handler_disconnect` -> `g_type_check_instance` **SIGSEGV**. **That is a use-after-free in mutter while destroying a disconnecting Wayland client** — a GObject already freed when the teardown tries to unhook its signal handler. `gnome-shell 46.0-0ubuntu6~24.04.14`, Ubuntu 24.04, VirtualBox VM, software GL. **This is the compositor’s bug, not TIDE’s**, and the distinction has been got wrong twice in this journal, so state it carefully both ways. **For TIDE:** the 18:28:59 crash landed within seconds of `TIDE_STANDALONE` being SIGTERM’d, and TIDE’s `gmpi-wl` memfd (`gmpi_ui/backends/DrawingFrameWayland.h:96`) was mapped in the compositor at the time. **Against TIDE:** `wl_display_flush_clients` iterates EVERY client and the core does not name which one was being destroyed; `/memfd:… (deleted)` is how every memfd appears, so it proves only that TIDE was connected; the 2026-08-21 16:48 crash happened with TIDE **not running at all**; and TIDE’s own backend handles a lost connection cleanly (`running_ = false`, `DrawingFrameWayland.h:3856-3868`) rather than faulting. **So: any client exiting can plausibly trigger it, TIDE is not special, and "the compositor crash IS S23’s 139" does NOT follow** — that path exits, it does not segfault. **Why this is worth a row rather than a journal bullet:** it has now cost two runs their runtime work, it destroys the developer’s session without warning, and every future linux run that launches `TIDE_STANDALONE` is exposed to it. **Options:** (a) tell Jeff, and let him decide about the VM’s graphics stack or a `gnome-shell` update — this is his desktop and not a run’s to change; (b) run GUI verification against a **nested/headless compositor** (`weston --backend=headless`, or a nested `wayland` session) so a compositor crash costs a scratch process and not the login session — this is the one a run can actually adopt; (c) accept it and keep runtime work short. **Nothing here is a code change to TIDE.** **Scope** the linux box’s environment, plus possibly a documented headless-compositor recipe under `docs/`. **Accept** a linux run can drive `TIDE_STANDALONE` for ten minutes without risking the desktop session — demonstrated once. **MET — see the measurement above.** **Size** small for (b); (a) is Jeff’s. |

Measured 2026-08-23 (linux, scheduled run, seventh item at Jeff's direction), branch `tide/linux/S37-clap-collision`.** S43(ii) landed, so the Linux CLAP draws — and `strace` on a clean `main` build now shows it reading `Resources/ControlsXp.xml`, `Resources/Prefabs/*.synthedit` and the rest, where this morning it read **nothing**. **THE HARM, IN THREE STEPS, in a shared install directory set up exactly as R4's `README.txt` instructs:** **(1) TIDE alone** — 6 prefabs, zero diagnostics. **(2) a second GMPI CLAP installed alongside** — TIDE loads **7** prefabs, one of them the OTHER PRODUCT'S, and the other plugin's same-named `ControlsXp.xml` overwrites TIDE's, which TIDE reports itself: `enriched 0 of 0 described class(es) -- ZERO`, so its own controls silently lose their pin descriptions. **(3) that plugin UNINSTALLED** — its uninstaller removes the files its package shipped, one of which is by then **TIDE's** `ControlsXp.xml`: `missing from bundle resources - those controls will have no pins`. **An unrelated plugin's uninstall leaves TIDE Rack permanently degraded, and nothing in TIDE can detect or prevent it.** **WHICH OPTION:** **(a) is not forbidden but rests on host behaviour unverifiable here** — `clap/entry.h` says hosts search *"for files and/or bundles as appropriate in your OS"*, the Linux convention is a bare `.so`, and the search is recursive, so a `TIDE-Rack.clap/` directory containing a `TIDE-Rack.clap` resolves differently per host. **(b) is host-independent and is the recommendation:** one function derives BOTH shared paths
