Measured 2026-08-23 (macos, scheduled run), branch `tide/mac/issue-310`.** [#310](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/issues/310) reported `main` red on macOS with `build/_deps/gmpi_wrappers-src/wrapper/AU2/AU2_Wrapper.cpp:8:10: fatal error: 'backends/GmpiObjCNames.h' file not found`. No TIDE commit was at fault. S38 shipped as two PRs in two repos, and TIDE fetches both by `GIT_TAG origin/main`: `GMPI_Wrappers#13` (the `#include`) landed at **08:03:41Z**, `gmpi_ui` `6aa8871` (the header) at **08:04:07Z** — **26 seconds apart** — and the failing run's own configure log straddles the gap: `08:03:42.10Z Fetching gmpi_ui from github` (25 s before the header existed) and `08:03:59.35Z dep GMPI_Wrappers [fetched]` (18 s after the `#include` did). It self-heals: `9806401b` (09:08) and `318d2748` (09:41) were both green on macOS with no fix in between, and `main` at `736e75b8` builds rc=0 with all five formats on this box today. **THIS IS NOT X4 RE-FILED** (WONTFIX — leave the pins alone) and it proposes no change to them. X4's reasoning contains the sentence this measurement corrects: *"CI clones fresh, so `origin/main` there is real"* — true **per repo**, false **across** repos, because CI resolves each sibling at a different instant and there is no snapshot that is consistent between them. The journal already carries *"a two-PR fix can have an ORDER, not just a pairing"* (GMPI#6/#274, GMPI_Wrappers#11/GMPI#8, and now this) — what is new is the measured cost when the order is right and the **interval** is not. **The damage is not the red build**, which nobody was waiting on; it is that `build.yml` files a `platform:{PLATFORM}` issue on failure, that issue is STEP 1 work outranking all backlog items, and the next run on each of three boxes spends its session re-verifying a break that fixed itself. **Options, none costed, and the cheap ones are not the pins:** (a) have the issue-filing step re-check the branch's latest run before filing, so a transient that has already gone green files nothing; (b) have it recognise a missing-header-from-a-fetched-sibling failure and say so in the issue body, so a run can dismiss it in one command instead of a build; (c) retry the configure once on a fetch-dependent failure. (a) is the one that removes the cost for all three platforms. **Scope** `.github/workflows/build.yml` — which is `.github/workflows/**` and therefore **Jeff's**, not a scheduled run's, per the run prompt's no-exceptions rule. **Accept** a deliberately-broken sibling pairing produces no issue once the following run is green, demonstrated on a real run rather than argued. **Size** small, and it needs Jeff because of the path. **BUILT AND DEMONSTRATED 2026-08-23 (windows, interactive, Jeff directing), branch `tide/win/S41-close-issue-on-green`. AND OPTION (a) DOES NOT WORK — it would have filed [#310](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/issues/310), the issue this row was written about.** Measured on the run timeline: the run that filed #310 started `08:02:56Z` and **was the newest run for `main` at that instant**; `main` went green at `09:08:46Z`, 64 minutes later. At filing time there is no following run to consult. **This row's own Accept describes the right mechanism and its options do not implement it** — *"no issue ONCE THE FOLLOWING RUN IS GREEN"* is a statement about a later run, so the step belongs on the later run. **What shipped is one step, `Close the platform issue on success`**, on the same matrix job, keyed on the same title the filing step builds; no new permission, the job already has `issues: write`. **Only issues this mechanism could have filed are touched** — exact title, platform label, and `author.is_bot`, checked against real data: #310 and #306 are `is_bot=true`, hand-filed #314 (tide-rack-bot) is `false`, so an agent's or a person's issue is never auto-closed. **DEMONSTRATED ON REAL RUNS, per the Accept:** a throwaway branch with a deliberate `message(FATAL_ERROR)` filed [#322](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/issues/322) (linux, `00:52:57Z`) and [#323](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/issues/323) (macos, `00:53:20Z`); reverting the break closed **#323 at `00:55:07Z` and #322 at `00:57:05Z`**, each by its own platform's job, **while the run was still in progress** — windows was still building. Third control, unplanned: the work branch's own green run logged *"No open CI-filed issue ... nothing to close"* rather than erroring. **TWO THINGS THE ISSUE HISTORY SAYS THAT THIS ROW DID NOT:** **31 issues were filed on 2026-08-20 alone**, all linux, one per branch, closed in a single manual sweep the next day — the de-duplication key is branch+platform, so one broken dependency files a fresh issue for every branch anyone pushes; and **#306 is still open for a branch that no longer exists** (`git ls-remote` returns nothing), which nothing will ever close. **KNOWN COST, stated in the workflow file:** the filing step de-duplicates on OPEN issues only, so a branch that flaps red/green/red now files a SECOND issue where it used to comment on the first. | E7 | TODO | any | **THE POLYPHONY MODEL IS RULED 2026-08-21, and it reframes this row rather than closing it.** Jeff: modules are **always monophonic** and the SynthEdit runtime **clones** them per voice, automatically, on the DSP graph only (not the GUI); the user sets a voice count on the MIDI-CV rack module, so *"polyphony is essentially 'free' as far as individual modules are concerned."* **So no TIDE module should be designed polyphonic.** **AND THE CONTAINER QUESTION IS ALREADY SOLVED — corrected by Jeff the same day, against this agent's first reading.** Jeff: *"conceptually they are rack modules, but in reality we place the MIDI-CV2 at the root level and route it into the Container (which represents it on the GUI as patch-points). This is already solved. MIDI-CV as rack module [is] how the end-user thinks of it, not how it is implemented."* **So root-placement-plus-Container-facade is the ARCHITECTURE, not a workaround**, and nothing in the MVP needs polyphony to cross a Container boundary. **The agent had flagged this as newly critical and was wrong**: it read the I/O-modules ruling (*they ARE rack modules, movable*) as putting MIDI-CV back inside a Container, when presentation and placement are deliberately separate. Recorded because the wrong inference is the tempting one — the row's own wording (*"polyphony cannot escape a container"*) invites it. **What remains of this row is the engine limitation as a fact, not as a blocker.** **Previous text follows.** **ROOT CAUSE OF THE INTERIM'S SILENCE FOUND, and it was neither of the two suspects this row listed — it was TIDE having no TYPE CONVERTERS.** Jeff: "the library will automatically insert converters when needed, so long as the converter is linked in." `ug_base.cpp:1751` builds an id like `SE <From>To<To>` for any mixed-datatype connection and calls `GetById()`; a miss is `assert(false); return;`, which in **Release** silently abandons the connection. `Converters.cpp` is a separate target's source, so it shipped only as a loadable `Converters.sem` and TIDE — static, no scan (S1a) — had **no `SE BoolToVolts`**, so `SE MIDItoGate2`'s `BoolOutPin` gate could never reach an audio-rate jack. Fixed by linking it into `SynthEditSem` with `my_type_convert.cpp` and staging `Converters.xml`; **the rack now plays the note** (`tests/hosts/v3-midi-gate.rpp`: silent, 440.0 Hz for the note's duration, silent). **This was a whole class of silent failure** — any mixed-datatype cable in TIDE was dead — so it is worth more than the row it unblocked. **What remains for E7 is unchanged and is now purely the POLYPHONY question:** `SE MIDI to CV 2` has the PITCH output the monophonic stand-in lacks, and it is still unusable because its container becomes a voice container. Jeff's rulings below already reduce that to "where do the jacks live". **Original row follows.** **CORRECTION 2026-08-18, and it narrows the remaining bug to one boundary. The row below says `SE MIDItoGate2` "emits no gate at all" — THAT IS WRONG.** It was an inference from a silent rack, never a direct measurement, and Jeff's question ("is MIDItoGate2 MIDI2 aware?") is what exposed it. **It is fully MIDI 2.0 compatible and it does set the gate.** Two proofs. **By code:** `MidiConverter2::processMidi` opens with an explicit pass-through for MIDI 2.0 input (`modules/se_sdk3/mp_midi.h:966`, *"MIDI 2.0 messages need no conversion — pass through and return"*), and `MidiToGate2::onMidi2Message` requires `ChannelVoice64`, which is exactly the message type TIDE forwards. **By a temporary stderr trace added to `modules/MidiPlayer2/MidiToGate.cpp` and then REVERTED unbuilt** (a temporary trace that got committed is issue [#87](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/issues/87); this one was not) — `MTG2: onMidiMessage pin=0 size=8 bytes=40 90 3c 00 isMidi2=1` · `MTG2: onMidi2Message msgType=4 status=9 (ChannelVoice64=4 NoteOn=9)` · `MTG2: NOTE ON note=60 -> pinGate=true triggerCounter=22` · `MTG2: trigger pulse ended at sample 6 -> setSleep(true)`. So it receives the UMP, decodes it, reads **note 60** — the fixture's middle C — and sets `pinGate = true`. `triggerCounter=22` is the 0.5 ms pulse at 44.1 kHz, correct. **WHERE THE BREAK ACTUALLY IS.** The rack downstream measured **zero for the entire render**, peak −inf, including the samples while the gate was set. So the gate is set on the pin and never arrives at the Envelope's ADSR. Note this does NOT overturn "a monophonic module's cable is live" — the cable demonstrably drove the GATE jack from its default of 10 down to 0, which is why the rack went silent. **The cable crosses; the VALUE does not.** Suspects, both concrete and neither yet tested: (1) `pinGate` is a `BoolOutPin` and the jack is an audio-rate `SE Patch Point in`, so a static/event bool may simply not drive an audio-rate signal — which would also explain why `--connect $mtg:Gate $pp:Input` is *accepted* by SynthEditCL while carrying nothing; (2) `setSleep(true)` fires 22 samples after the note-on while the gate is still logically HIGH, so a sleeping module may stop driving its output entirely. **Test (1) first** — it is a datatype question answerable with SynthEditCL alone, no GUI: put a `Bool to Volts`-style conversion (or a `Multiply`) between the two and see whether the gate arrives. If it does, the shipped interim just needs that module inside the prefab. **Original row follows.** **UPDATED 2026-08-18 (macos, interactive, Jeff directing) — the cause is now confirmed EMPIRICALLY as well as architecturally, and Jeff's rulings settle most of the design.** **(a) Expansion is purely mechanical** — lifted content behaves exactly as if it had been put in the parent in the first place; the exceptions are cosmetic, e.g. a per-container CPU readout must sum its modules and present the total as a pretence. That removes the one real objection to covert expansion, which was whether it would make MIDI-CV 2 a genuine root-level voice source. It would. **(b) The "two on one rack" problem dissolves:** a MIDI-CV is close to mandatory in an instrument, so add ONE automatically to every new project and remove the option to add more — project furniture rather than something the user places, costing neither a serialised flag nor a name-matching convention. **Shape: one implicit root-level MIDI-CV per project, the rack module reduced to the jacks that expose it. STILL TO DECIDE: where those jacks live**, since PITCH and GATE must be cabled from somewhere. **THE CAUSE, MEASURED.** `SE MIDItoGate2` is monophonic, so it does not make its container a voice container. A rack cabled from it renders **digital silence** — and the silence is the proof, because the Envelope's GATE jack defaults to 10 (open), so a rack that goes SILENT when that jack is cabled has a **live cable driving it to 0**. Cables out of a monophonic container cross; out of a polyphonic one they do not. That is the experiment the original row below asked for, answered. **GETTING IT LINKED, AND A WRONG ANSWER WORTH RECORDING.** `SE MIDItoGate2` was absent from TIDE. **It is NOT the `#if GMPI_IS_PLATFORM_JUCE==1` block around `INIT_STATIC_FILE(MIDItoGate)`** — wrong twice over: `INIT_STATIC_FILE(ADSR)` is inside that same block while ADSR works fine in TIDE (TIDE's ADSR is the legacy `ug_adsr.cpp` `REGISTER_MODULE_1` one, reached through the legacy table), and adding an unconditional `INIT_STATIC_FILE(MIDItoGate)` **fails to link** — `se_static_library_init_MIDItoGate()` undefined. **Real reason:** `modules/MidiPlayer2/MidiToGate.cpp` is in a `SynthEditLib/CMakeLists.txt` source list belonging to a **separate `MidiPlayer2` target**, so it builds as a LOADABLE module and is not in the static library — `MidiToGate.o` lands under `MidiPlayer2.build/`, `MidiToCv2.o` under `SynthEditLib.build/`. TIDE links statically and has no scan (S1a). **Fixed with NO GATED change:** the `.cpp` joined `SynthEditSem/CMakeLists.txt`'s source list, `MidiPlayer2.xml` staged for its pins — E2a's `RectangleGui.cpp` pattern — and **`MIDI to Gate2` now appears in TIDE's browser**, the authoritative check. **WHY THE INTERIM STILL DOES NOT SOUND — delivery is ruled out.** It emits no gate at all: 0 for the whole render, note present, `TIDE: host MIDI reaching the rack` printed. **PROBE D proves MIDI crosses the patch cable out of the `MIDI In` module.** Jeff asked whether that had been proved and it had not: PROBE A (module present AND cabled) gates while PROBE B (module absent) does not, but that moved two variables, leaving live the possibility that the module's mere PRESENCE makes the container a MIDI destination and `ug_container`'s redirector feeds MIDI-CV 2 directly (`ug_base.cpp:2859`). **PROBE D holds the module present with the pin UNCONNECTED: gate flat 0.** A and D differ only in the cable, only A gates, so the cable is the carrier. Not a threshold either — measured, the ADSR opens identically at 1 V and 10 V. **Suspects now inside the module only:** the `setSleep(true)` / `subProcessNothing` path in `MidiToGate2::subProcess` and the cross-class `setSubProcess(&MidiToGate::subProcessNothing)` beside it, both at `modules/MidiPlayer2/MidiToGate.cpp:222`. All four probes run from `build-prefabs.py --diagnostics`. **Original row follows.** **A rack module that is a POLYPHONIC SOURCE does not drive other rack modules through a patch cable — found 2026-08-18 (macos, interactive, Jeff directing) while building V3, and it is what blocks V3 rather than anything about MIDI.** **What is proven.** Host MIDI reaches the rack: TIDE's new check prints `TIDE: host MIDI reaching the rack - first message 8 byte(s), status 0x40` (a MIDI 2.0 UMP, GMPI's `DT_MIDI2`) — [SynthEdit#46](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/SynthEdit/pull/46). `SE MIDI to CV 2` converts it correctly: with a `Sound Out` INSIDE its own container, its **Gate rises at 0.500 s and falls at 1.200 s**, exactly the fixture's note. **But the same signal taken out through a patch cable produces digital silence** — `tests/hosts/v1-rack-midi.rpp`, four cables, renders **identically with and without the note** (peak −6.3 dBFS, 440.0 Hz throughout), and 440.0 Hz is the *Oscillator's own* 5 V default, so both MIDI cables contributed nothing. A two-module probe (MIDI PITCH → Output L, a jack with **no** stored default, so it would have passed anything that arrived) renders **−inf**. **CAUSE CONFIRMED by Jeff, 2026-08-18, from the architecture rather than from another measurement — this is a PRE-EXISTING constraint, not a new defect: polyphony cannot escape a container.** `SE MIDI to CV 2` is `polyphonicSource="true"` / `cloned="true"` (`MidiToCv2.cpp:18`), so its container is a voice container, and a voice container's outputs do not cross out. **The other half of his answer resolves what looked like a contradiction:** a container with NO MIDI-CV in it is **expanded** on the DSP side already — it is effectively cosmetic, like grouping objects in a paint program — so V1's oscillator→envelope→output cables are just wires in one flat graph, which is exactly why they work. **The rule that follows: as long as the MIDI-CV is not inside a sub-container, the rest of the patch works fine, containers or not.** So the earlier "cable a non-polyphonic source out of a container" experiment is unnecessary and has been dropped from this row — the answer was architectural, not empirical. **TWO CANDIDATE SOLUTIONS, Jeff's, and the choice is his to make.** **(1) Covert expansion:** special-case this one rack module so the DSP side lifts its contents into the parent with every wire destination rerouted, which needs an extra flag serialised on that container. **(2) Convention:** the rack module becomes nothing but a container of patch-points (Trigger, Gate, Pitch...), and any container shaped like that gets a secretly-added `MIDI In` + `MIDI-CV 2` pair wired in at the root level. **A first read, not a ruling:** (1) reuses expansion machinery that already runs on every other container and keeps the module honest in the structure view — you can open it and see what it is, which is how this bug got diagnosed at all; (2) needs no new serialised state but identifies the container by the NAMES of its patch points, and names are the least stable thing in a document. Both have to answer "what happens with two MIDI rack modules on one rack". **Scope** likely `SynthEditLib` (GATED) — the expansion path and the voice/clone machinery — plus a generator change in `TideModules/`. **Accept** `tests/hosts/v1-rack-midi.rpp` renders audio that starts at 0.500 s and stops at 1.200 s. The fixture already exists and already fails, so this is a re-measure, not a new test. **Size** unknown; the two designs differ enough that it wants Jeff's pick first. **Do not start by suspecting the prefabs or the MIDI path** — both are measured good, and the probes behind `build-prefabs.py --diagnostics` re-run that proof in one command. |
| V2 | BLOCKED | any | Host-automatable parameters without a panel view (design note 4 in [docs/design-notes.md](docs/design-notes.md)). **This premise is now stale too — panel view (the rack) is TIDE's default view as of the 2026-08-13 pivot, not something being avoided. Design note 4 needs re-reading against that before this row is trusted; not done here, flagged in passing while splitting E2.** **SHAPE ESTABLISHED 2026-08-18 from how every competitor solves this — [docs/competitive-review.md](docs/competitive-review.md) §3. The field splits in two and the answer is not close.** RNBO (`[param name]`), plugdata and Bitwig's Grid expose parameters **declaratively from the patch with no panel required**; Reaktor and Max for Live make you draw a knob first, and **Reaktor's Multi-Picture controls cannot be automated at all** while its auto-generated automation IDs are not auto-maintained, so deleting a control leaves holes. **The two serious DAW-hosted racks both use a pre-declared slot pool** — VCV Rack Pro **1024 slots**, Reason Rack **>2000 advertised** (~256 practically automatable, UNVERIFIED/community-sourced) — bound automatically as modules are added, which is what makes them work with zero user setup. **Both then made the same mistake, and it is the one thing this row must not repeat: the binding is POSITIONAL.** It follows insertion order and a recycled free list, so deleting a module orphans the DAW's automation lane and **the slot may be reused by a newly created module's parameter, silently driving the wrong thing** — documented in VCV *and* Reason. Reason additionally pushes parameter names so lazily that the known Ableton workaround is *dragging the device a few pixels inside the plugin* to make names populate; its sanctioned answer is "wrap it in a Combinator and automate that", i.e. it deferred the problem and shipped a panel builder to cover it. **So the ruling: (1) pre-declare a pool so automation needs no setup; (2) bind slots to a persistent content-addressed identity — module UUID + parameter id — NEVER to insertion order or a free list; (3) push names to the host eagerly.** **TIDE is unusually well placed for (2)** because S11 already puts the whole document in the plugin state with stable module ids. **Cheap now, near-impossible to retrofit once patches exist in the wild.** **One open position worth noting while here:** the declarative camp pays for it with generic auto-generated panels (RNBO's real UI means writing C++/JUCE), and the panel-first camp taxes the user to build one — **nobody ships automatic host parameters AND a good default panel AND an optional custom one.** Since the 2026-08-13 pivot the rack panel *is* TIDE's default UI, so that combination is nearly free here. |

Note `voice_midi_note` also contains the naive oscillator yet passes at DEFAULT gates (−123.1 dBFS on macOS), so "naive drifts" is not the whole story either — its pitch is MIDI-derived rather than the free-running default, which is the obvious variable to isolate first. **Why it matters:** a −67 dB gate on a case whose true residual is −121 dB leaves ~55 dB of margin for a real regression to hide in, and E1a's own reasoning is that a gate wide enough to be safe is a gate narrow enough to be useless. **Scope** `tests/cases/prefab_oscillator.json` and `tests/cases/prefab_filter.json` — the tolerance triple in each. **Accept** the two cases run at gates justified by a measurement of *their own* residual across mac and Linux (both directions, since a reference seeded on one platform is the asymmetric case), with the numbers and the platform pair recorded in `tolerance_reason` as E1a did; and a positive control showing the tightened gates still pass on both platforms while failing a deliberately introduced regression. **Do not simply drop to the defaults without measuring** — that is the same reasoning-instead-of-measuring this row is about. **Size** small; the measurement is one CI run plus one local run per case. |

It was NOT written by the run that filed this row, is on no branch and has no commit history, and **it was deliberately left untouched**. **Jeff's status for it, given 2026-08-18: *"we're spit-balling with the marathon stuff"* — it is exploratory, and this row treats the two proposals as PEERS rather than as a baseline and a comment.** The Marathon document is worked out in more detail; **detail is not validation**, and the Blocks-derived proposal is the one anchored to the brief Jeff actually gave and to five shipping UIs in this category. **The two conflict on exactly the point Jeff raised.** **(a) The Marathon/Designers-Republic language** ([docs/ui-design-language.md](docs/ui-design-language.md)): true-black ground, flat `#1C1C1C` panels where the tone step *is* the depth cue, 1px butt-cap seams, acid `#C0FE04` used **only for live state**, per-family livery hues, knobs as arcs, right-angled polyline cables, stepped edges as the signature device — and **"banned outright: gradients as decoration, drop shadows, blur, glow, bevels"**. Its strongest argument is practical: it is a pure 2D language drawable in GMPI code from plain rects, lines and arcs with **no bitmap assets**, which suits constraint 8 and the sandbox, and its own example SVGs double as a feasibility proof. **Its standing risk, which the document itself raises and then guardrails against: the source language's own shipped UI was widely panned for legibility (players coined "fontslop"), and the failure mode named was density on a surface people read continuously — which is exactly what a plugin is.** That caution is not disqualifying, but it is the thing to test before adopting, and it does not apply to (b). **(b) The Blocks-derived language** ([docs/competitive-review.md](docs/competitive-review.md) §5), from five competitor UIs **viewed directly as screenshots, not recalled**: flat matte panels carrying **dimensional controls — top-lit gradient, faint sheen, and a soft low-opacity contact shadow reading as ambient occlusion** — with hue encoding signal type, cables thin/behind/hideable, and inline dataviz. **The crux, stated plainly: Jeff's brief said he likes Reaktor Blocks because it is "abstract but with some tasteful realism like shadows". Proposal (a) bans shadows. Both cannot be satisfied and this is not an agent's call.** **It wants a `PROPOSED:` entry in [docs/decisions.md](docs/decisions.md).** `PROPOSED: which visual design language does TIDE Rack adopt? Options: (a) the Marathon/TDR language in docs/ui-design-language.md, flat with no shadows; (b) the Blocks-derived language in competitive-review §5, flat panels with dimensional controls and soft contact shadows; (c) a synthesis led by (b) — (b)'s material direction and signal-type colour, harvesting from (a) everything that does not conflict: the no-bitmap drawability discipline, 1px butt-cap geometry, a deliberately restricted palette, and accent-as-state rather than accent-as-decoration. Recommended default: (c). (b) supplies the direction because it is what Jeff named liking and it is validated by five shipping UIs in this category rather than imported from a game whose own UI was panned for legibility; (a) supplies craft discipline (b) does not attempt, and a contact shadow is a few alpha steps rather than a bitmap, so nothing in (a)'s drawability argument is given up by admitting it. Default in effect meanwhile: none — TIDE renders SynthEdit's developer UI. May proceed meanwhile: E2a's three prefabs, whose panels are throwaway under any option. Decide-by: before E2 authors the curated set.` **Whoever takes this reads both in full first.** [docs/ui-design-language.md](docs/ui-design-language.md) is the more specified on palette, geometry, type and density — real gaps in §5 that any adopted language has to close, whichever direction wins. [docs/competitive-review.md](docs/competitive-review.md) §5 is the one carrying the evidence: five shipping competitor UIs viewed directly, the density failure that sinks dense cabled racks, and the authoring-cost argument for a small team across 30–60 modules. **The characterisation Jeff's preference needs to be implementable:** in Blocks the realism is **not in the panel** (flat, matte, ~`#f0f0ee`, no screws, no texture, no bevel) but **entirely in the controls** — grey cylinder, subtle top-lit vertical gradient, faint specular sheen, and a **soft low-opacity contact shadow** that reads as ambient occlusion rather than a cast shadow. **The most useful negative result in the survey:** Voltage Modular proves **thin cables do not save a dense rack** — its panels are unreadable under the patch — which argues for hideable cables plus the Grid's show-modulation-at-the-destination model. **An anchor already exists:** the website's accent `#1c6e8c` light / `#6ec2dc` dark on `#101216` is both tide-coloured and close to Blocks' cyan. **Scope** a decisions.md entry, then a spec doc. **Accept** a ruling exists that E2 can author panels against. **Size** the survey is done; the ruling is Jeff's. **STATUS CORRECTION, 2026-08-18 (later session, Jeff directing):** the "uncommitted, on no branch, no commit history" description above is no longer accurate — [docs/ui-design-language.md](docs/ui-design-language.md) and its now-**four** generated SVGs are committed on `tide/win/competitive-review`, together with [scripts/gen-ui-language-svgs.py](scripts/gen-ui-language-svgs.py) which regenerates them. **This does not decide E17** — it commits proposal (a) so it can be read and diffed, and E17 remains NEEDS-JEFF. **One detail above is now stale in a way that matters to the crux:** (a) is described as having "right-angled polyline cables", but Jeff directed the opposite that session — cables are now **fat, curved, physical objects with round plug heads**, an explicit move toward the grounded realism the row attributes to (b). Shadows are still banned in (a), so the conflict stands, but it is narrower than "(a) refuses all realism". Also added under Jeff's direction: plate text, quarter-turn labels, corner-crosshair grouping in place of section borders, a 70%-size unit tier, and a lifted label ink for long-session legibility. **Read (a) as it now stands, not as this row describes it.** |

## Release & distribution — UNBLOCKED 2026-08-21 (V1 closed 08-18, C7 closed 08-21)

The plan is [docs/distribution.md](docs/distribution.md). R1 is the only item
with lead time and the only one Jeff can do now; the rest wait for a v0.1 that
passes V1. CI automation additionally waits on C7 — the interim path (build on
each box, `gh release upload`) is in the doc.

| R1 | RESOLVED | — | **Signing identities and accounts.** **(a) Decided 2026-08-13 by Jeff: TIDE ships under the existing `SynthEdit Limited` identity, deliberately — a second Azure certificate profile is not affordable right now.** So both platforms sign under `SynthEdit Limited`/`SynthEditCertificateProfile` as already configured; no new credential, no R7-style hardening needed for a profile that doesn't exist. Compensating branding decided the same day: **"TIDE Synth — by SynthEdit Ltd"**, placed subtly (about pane, footer, or installer credit — not the plugin name itself, constraints 1 and 5 still rule out anything dialog- or splash-shaped). Placement is unscoped — filed as **D2**. (b) **Confirmed** — Apple Developer ID is `Developer ID Application: SynthEdit Limited (36SNPLRFK3)`, and notarization runs on the app-specific-password route (`APPLE_ID` / `APPLE_ID_PASSWORD` / `APPLE_TEAM_ID`), matching `SynthEdit_cmake_mac.yml:223-244` so the recipe ports without rewriting `notarytool`. An App Store Connect API key would be scoped and revocable where the app-specific password is tied to Jeff's Apple ID and grants far more than notarization — worth switching before anything consumes it, not urgent while nothing does. (c) App Store listing for the iOS AUv3 (needed by M2, not v0.1) — **not started.** (d) **Done early, ahead of C7** — 8 Actions secrets and 4 variables now exist on the public repo: secrets `AZURE_TENANT_ID`, `AZURE_CLIENT_ID`, `AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET`, `APPLE_CERT_P12_BASE64`, `APPLE_CERT_PASSWORD`, `APPLE_ID`, `APPLE_ID_PASSWORD`, `APPLE_TEAM_ID`; variables `AZURE_CODESIGN_ENDPOINT`, `AZURE_CODESIGN_ACCOUNT`, `AZURE_CODESIGN_PROFILE`, `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY`. The non-secret half is deliberately in *variables* — those values are on every binary TIDE ships, and hiding them only makes failures harder to read. Nothing consumes any of them yet. **Two follow-ups, filed as R7.** Detail on the identities, and why the non-secret half is deliberately in variables: [docs/r1-signing-identities.md](docs/r1-signing-identities.md). |

But it also runs on `push: branches: ["main", "tide/**"]`, and same-repo runs get full secret access — and pushing `tide/**` branches is exactly what the weekly agents do. | R8 | DONE | mac | **DONE — [#289](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/289) and [GMPI#9](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/GMPI/pull/9) merged; flipped 2026-08-22 (macos) as STEP 4 bookkeeping, PR state verified.** **And `com.tidesynth.tiderack.au` is no longer latent** — `AU` joined `FORMATS_LIST` in the same run, so all five identifiers are live and measured on the artifacts. **Previous text follows.** **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 (macos, Jeff directing), branch `tide/mac/R8-bundle-identifiers` plus [GMPI#9](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/GMPI/pull/9) for the AU half.** Scheme, following R9's ruling that TIDE owns its identity: **reverse-DNS on `tidesynth.com`, per format** — `com.tidesynth.tiderack.gmpi` / `.vst3` / `.clap` / `.au`, and the standalone takes the bare `com.tidesynth.tiderack` because it is the application rather than one of the plug-in formats. **Distinct per format on purpose:** all of them can be installed side by side and macOS treats the identifier as the unit of identity, so sharing one is the same class of collision R9 just removed from the plug-in id. **MEASURED ON THE ARTIFACTS, and the proof this row was about is the last line:** all five bundles carry their identifier from a plain build, and `codesign` now reports `Identifier=com.tidesynth.tiderack.vst3` where it used to invent `TIDE-Rack-a330dda6894e6d221c12682d5774c181b4845f8b` from a hash. **Two different causes, two different fixes.** The plug-in formats had the key **present and EMPTY** — CMake's stock `MacOSXBundleInfo.plist.in` substitutes `MACOSX_BUNDLE_GUI_IDENTIFIER`, which GMPI sets for STANDALONE only — so TIDE now sets it per target in its own [SynthEditSem/CMakeLists.txt](SynthEditSem/CMakeLists.txt), ALLOWED and no upstream change needed. **The AU was different: the key was ABSENT**, because its plist comes from `plist_util` rather than CMake and the emitting block was `#if 0`'d out over a *"bundle ID not matching"* warning. GMPI#9 passes `--bundle-id "$<TARGET_PROPERTY:...,MACOSX_BUNDLE_GUI_IDENTIFIER>"`, so any plugin that declares one gets it and one that does not is unaffected. **NEGATIVE CONTROL RUN on that claim:** `plist_util` with `--bundle-id ""` emits **no** `CFBundleIdentifier` key, with a real one it emits exactly one — so the GMPI change is a no-op for every existing caller rather than a new default. **The AU path was re-validated end to end** with the new identifier AND R9's new `Drck` subtype: registers, and `auval` reports `AU VALIDATION SUCCEEDED`, rc=0. **`com.tidesynth.tiderack.au` is LATENT until M1's last step** — `AU` is still not in `FORMATS_LIST`, deliberately kept out of this change so a bisect can separate "we turned on AU" from "we set identifiers". **NOT verified: notarization.** This removes the synthesised identifier that R5 would have met, but no submission has been made, so whether `notarytool` is now happy is still one submission away. **Previous text follows.** **No plugin bundle TIDE ships has a `CFBundleIdentifier`, and `codesign` papers over it with a synthesised one.** Found 2026-08-22 (macos) while checking whether CLAP (R4a) had a plist problem — it does not, it has the *same* problem as everything else, which is what makes this pre-existing rather than a regression. **Measured on all four bundles from a clean Release build, and the accurate wording matters:** `TIDE-Rack.clap`, `TIDE-Rack.vst3` and `TIDE-Rack.gmpi` each have a `CFBundleIdentifier` key that is **present and EMPTY** (`<string></string>`) — not absent. Only `TIDE-Rack.app` has a real one (`com.gmpi.standalone.TIDE_Rack`). **One cause explains both symptoms:** the plugin formats fall through to CMake's stock `MacOSXBundleInfo.plist.in`, and `MACOSX_BUNDLE_GUI_IDENTIFIER` is only ever set on the STANDALONE branch (`GMPI/gmpi_plugin.cmake:775`), so the template's substitution yields an empty string — and the same stock template is why all four also declare `CFBundlePackageType=APPL`, the *application* type, rather than `BNDL`. **So the fix is one property per format, not a new plist.** **What actually happens, tested rather than predicted:** `codesign --force --sign -` SUCCEEDS and invents an identifier from the executable name plus a hash — observed as `TIDE-Rack-555549444341029c5cd537c59001e63d20c200d3`. So this does not block signing outright. **Why it still matters, and to whom:** that identifier is the unit Gatekeeper, notarization tickets and any future update or uninstall logic key on, and a name-plus-hash string matches no convention TIDE controls. **This is R5's to hit** — R5 wires notarization, and `notarytool` is stricter than `codesign`. **Do not fix blind.** The Accept is: decide the identifier scheme (`com.synthedit.tiderack.vst3` / `.clap` / `.gmpi` is the obvious shape, but the publisher is *SynthEdit Limited* per R1, so it is a naming decision Jeff owns), then set `MACOSX_BUNDLE_GUI_IDENTIFIER` per format. **The plumbing is GMPI's, and PR-GATED** — `:774` sets it for STANDALONE only, so extending it to the plugin formats is a GMPI change. **NOT verified:** whether `notarytool` actually rejects a synthesised identifier. That is one submission away and R5 will find out; this row exists so R5 is not surprised by it. |

The commented-out original two lines further down still reads `<Plugin id="SE SynthEdit" name="SynthEdit" ...>`, which is where the value came from. **This is not cosmetic, and it is proven end to end rather than argued.** `GMPI_Wrappers/wrapper/VST3/MyVstPluginFactory.cpp:200` (`textIdtoUuid`) builds the VST3 class GUID as the ASCII `"PluginGMPI "`, a `P`/`C` role byte, and **`hashString(id)`** — a djb2 hash of the id string, nothing else. `hashString("SE SynthEdit")` = `0x43ED5119`, whose little-endian bytes are **`1951ED43`** — **exactly the trailing four bytes of the GUID in the committed fixture** `1386065673{506C7567696E474D504920501951ED43}` ([tests/hosts/v1-rack.rpp](tests/hosts/v1-rack.rpp)). So the shipped artifact's identity is a pure function of that string. `Factory_CLAP.cpp:25` assigns the same string straight to `clap_descriptor.id`, and GMPI uses it as the id directly. **Measured, not inferred:** a CLAP host handshake reports `id="SE SynthEdit" name="TIDE Rack" vendor="TIDE Synth"` — see [tests/e9_clap_rate_probe.c](tests/e9_clap_rate_probe.c). **What breaks:** any other GMPI plugin declaring that id gets the SAME VST3 GUID and the SAME CLAP id, so a host with both installed cannot tell them apart and a saved project can reload the wrong one. Uniqueness currently depends on nobody else using SynthEdit's own shell id — which is not a property TIDE controls. **WHY THE TIMING MATTERS AND WHY THIS IS NOT A QUIET FIX:** the GUID is a pure function of the id, so **changing the id changes the VST3 GUID**, and every project already saved with TIDE Rack stops finding the plugin. the two shell packagers' fallbacks now match. **VERIFIED by building:** all four macOS bundles — `.vst3`, `.clap`, `.gmpi` and the AUv3 app — report **0.1.1** where the same build previously reported `1.0.0`; the tag-strip was exercised (`v0.1.1` → `0.1.1`). **NOT VERIFIED:** no packager was executed (they need signing identities and a release environment), so the version reaching the installer names is by inspection, not by run; Windows and Linux packaging were not exercised at all. **NOTE this only works because [GMPI#12](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/GMPI/pull/12) landed** — before it, CMake picked a MODULE source for the plugin XML and never saw the version attribute, which is exactly what the first build after this change showed against a stale local GMPI. **Scope** `SynthEditSem/SynthEdit.cpp`, `scripts/package-*.sh`, `release.yml`. **Accept** a tagged release produces artifacts whose reported version matches the tag. **Size** done, pending review. |

A bare DLL is a legal VST3, but it has nowhere to keep the four pin XMLs and the six rack prefabs TIDE cannot work without, and shipping those loose into the SHARED `Common Files\VST3` folder would put them beside every other vendor's plug-ins. The bundle solves it exactly and the runtime already reads one: `BundleInfo.cpp:670-684` sets `pluginIsBundle` from "a path element with an extension followed by `Contents`", and `getResourceFolder()` (`:266-271`) then returns `<bundle>\Contents\Resources\`. **Verified in three conditions on this box, not argued:** (a) the flat DLL as the build leaves it -- *"no Prefabs folder in bundle resources"* and all four XMLs *"missing"*; (b) the same binary with those resources beside it -- 4 XMLs enriched, **6 rack prefab(s) seeded**; (c) **the shipped bundle, unzipped from the asset itself** -- 4 XMLs enriched, **6 rack prefab(s) seeded**, `rack built for 48000 Hz`. (b) proves the runtime rule and (c) proves the asset obeys it; (a) is the control that makes both mean something. **The installer is proven, not merely compiled:** `-SelfTest` recompiles the SAME `.iss` with two `#define`s overridden (`Vst3Dir` -> a scratch folder, `PrivilegesLevel` -> `lowest`, so no elevation is needed) and runs it silently -- **11 files installed, every one SHA-256 identical to the payload, and uninstall removes the bundle whole.** Windows has no `installer -target <sandbox>`, which is why the destination is a compile-time define rather than a runtime hook. **Signing is deliberately NOT done and NOT claimed.** The Azure Trusted Signing block runs only when `AZURE_TENANT_ID`/`AZURE_CLIENT_ID`/`AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET` are set, and it **refuses to proceed** rather than silently skip if credentials are present without `TRUSTED_SIGNING_DLIB`. It is **unverified**: `ArtifactSigning@1` is an Azure Pipelines task with no local or GitHub Actions equivalent, so this drives `signtool.exe` with the Azure Code Signing dlib instead -- the documented stand-in, which nobody has run. **R5 owns the secret store and should treat that block as a starting point to test.** **Found while packaging and filed as [S36](#), not fixed here:** on Windows the build stages those same resources one directory ABOVE the binary, so a developer's own build has an empty module browser. That is condition (a) above. **Also needed and recorded:** `cmake -S . -B build` picks the VS **BuildTools** instance on this box, which has no MFC, and the build dies on `afxres.h` in two `SynthEditLib/EditorLib` files. `-DCMAKE_GENERATOR_INSTANCE="C:/Program Files/Microsoft Visual Studio/18/Community"` fixes it; `docs/building.md` records that flag only for the old SE16 path. **Previous text follows.** **UNBLOCKED 2026-08-21 (macos, [#270](https://github.com/JeffMcClintock/TideSynth/pull/270)) — BOTH stated blockers cleared and nobody had noticed.** This section's header read *"blocked on V1 (nothing to ship yet)"*; **V1 closed 2026-08-18** with every clause measured. **C7 closed 2026-08-21** — the carve-out is complete and all three platforms build from a clean public clone. **And there is now something to ship**: N1a renamed the artifacts to their shipped forms, so `TIDE-Rack.vst3` / `.gmpi` / `.app` exist under the names [docs/distribution.md](docs/distribution.md) specifies, verified in REAPER. **The publisher name TIDE ships under is SynthEdit Limited — decided, not
inherited, as of R1(a) 2026-08-13.** `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY` is
`Developer ID Application: SynthEdit Limited (36SNPLRFK3)`, and
`SynthEditCertificateProfile` almost certainly carries the same subject on
Windows. So a user installing TIDE sees *SynthEdit Limited* in the macOS
Gatekeeper prompt and the Windows UAC dialog — deliberately: a second Azure
certificate profile is not affordable right now, and PR #14's website
stripped every SynthEdit mention so TIDE stands on its own there, which is
why a compensating credit was filed as **D2** — "TIDE Synth — by SynthEdit
Ltd", placed on TIDE's own terms rather than only surfacing in the OS
install dialog.

## Done

Landed rows live in **[BACKLOG-DONE.md](BACKLOG-DONE.md)** — moved there by A8,
2026-08-12. Look there for precedent: how a past stage was verified, what a
previous run measured.

**As part of STEP 4:** an `IN-REVIEW` row whose PRs have all merged becomes
`DONE` and moves to that file, with the merge date in the `Done` column. Move
the row verbatim; do not rewrite it on the way out.
